# FORGE — Technical Debt Tracker

Single source of truth for open and resolved tech debt. `AGENTS.md` points here
rather than duplicating this list — if you find a known-limitations note in
`AGENTS.md` that isn't reflected here, that's a sign the two have drifted; update
this file and trim `AGENTS.md` back down to a pointer.

Mentions of `CLAUDE.md` inside dated debt rows are historical references to the
legacy file that existed when those findings or resolutions were recorded.

When you find a new issue: add it here with the next available ID, don't fix it
inline unless it's trivial and unambiguously safe. Per the standing rule "design
before patching," anything structural gets a design conversation before code,
even if you could technically patch it in five minutes.

When you resolve one: move it to the Resolved table with the commit hash, don't
just delete the row — the history of what broke and how it got fixed is useful on
its own, especially for spotting repeat patterns (see "Common bug patterns" in
`AGENTS.md` and the detailed lessons in `MEMORY.md`).

---

## What GREEN means (standing rule — Raj, 2026-07-20)

> An area is GREEN only when it is CORRECT and HONEST. Correctness first, honesty
> always. A wrong answer honestly labelled is still a wrong answer. A defect in
> what a stage MEASURES is blocking, not quality debt, even when the reporting
> around it is impeccable.

**Retroactive:** every area currently scored was scored on HONESTY ALONE, so **no
current GREEN has been verified for correctness.** The measurement defects found
2026-07-20 (TD-162 StrategyDetector zero-count; TD-163 appType `spaDom=1`
non-discrimination) are the first of this class and are BLOCKING under this rule,
not quality debt — the reporting around them (ADR-020 grading) is impeccable and
they are still wrong. Correctness re-verification of every current GREEN is owed.

| TD-107 | TC069 (`e2e-journey.spec.ts` + `migrated/tc069-e2e-journey.spec.ts`) asserted protected-page inaccessibility via `expect(guestPage.url()).not.toContain('<page>.html')` **immediately after** `guestPage.goto('.../<page>.html')`. Wrong signal for SauceDemo: an unauthenticated direct-nav does **not** redirect the URL — the page stays on `/<page>.html` and renders an access-denied banner (`Epic sadface: You can only access '/<page>.html' when you are logged in`). `goto()` resolves before any client-side redirect, so the URL check both (a) asserts the wrong thing and (b) races → intermittent CI red (observed live: the `migrated` variant passed while `e2e-journey` failed in the *same* run). Surfaced by the TD-015 triage — a test defect, not an auth defect. | Medium | Fix (TD-015 triage follow-up): assert the banner (`expect(guestPage.locator('text=You can only access')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 3000 })`) at all three direct-nav blocks (inventory/cart/checkout) in both files, in place of the URL check. **Keep** the post-*login* `not.toContain('inventory.html')` check (line ~25/177) — that one verifies the blocked login didn't redirect and is correct. |

Fix: replace with ARIA roles (`[role="menuitem"]` etc., already partly present) and generic nav patterns; drop the app-specific class. | Medium | Surfaced during the TD-128 triage. The isSpa:false→hybrid rescue depends on this hardcoding today. |

Deferred from TD-128 — implement as a capability improvement, not a framework fingerprint patch. | Medium | The real fix for OrangeHRM-style SPAs with no framework fingerprint. The evidence-gate policy (`isAutonomyEligible = origin==='observed' \|\| deriveGoalGrounding(actions)==='observed'`; fail-toward-supervision, never grants more autonomy than the operator's flag) is clean + contained BUT governs nothing until a real supervision mechanism exists. | TD-141 | **Text-strategy match-semantics divergence between verification and generation.** `VerificationRunner.strategyToSelector` emits `text="v"` (QUOTED = exact match) while the canonical `EmitHelper.strategyToSelector` emits `text=v` (UNQUOTED = substring match). Verification and generated code therefore locate text-strategy elements by DIFFERENT semantics — verify can certify an element the generated test resolves differently (or not at all). Silent divergence; data-test/id/css/role (incl. guard + escaping) are identical. **Blocks the strategyToSelector consolidation**: the two must be reconciled to ONE ruled semantic BEFORE merging, or consolidation freezes the wrong behavior. | Medium | Found by the 2026-07-18 code-dust sweep. Needs a semantic ruling (exact vs substring) — a Nova design call, not a mechanical fix. Related: TD-095 (the other deliberately-un-unified precedence). |

| TD-144 | **Expand the authType detector vocabulary to recognize SSO / redirect-based auth.** The shipped authType detector's vocabulary is {form-login, none} (password-field presence); it cannot observe SSO/OIDC/SAML. Add recognition signals: cross-origin navigation on login attempt, IdP host signatures, OIDC/SAML redirect parameter shapes. This is Option A — a CAPABILITY milestone, NOT an honesty fix (ADR-019 already reports 'inconclusive' honestly for SSO-configured apps). | Medium · NB | **Explicit acceptance bar: must clear its own evaluation harness with real verification before it ships — it must NOT be smuggled in as part of an urgent fix.** Until then the ADR-019 competence gate reports inconclusive honestly, which is a declared limitation, not a defect — a declared limitation does not score its area RED (hence · NB). Successor to TD-142 (the honesty fix); this is the capability that would let authType actually be compared for SSO apps. |
| TD-145 | **ForgeStreamingReporter and the AI-pipeline summary report contradictory statuses for the same run.** Observed on CI run 29658308368 (`1b453c7`): the reporter logged `Run 2026-07-18T19-43-13 -> COMPLETED (status: failed)` while the AI pipeline reported `Passed: 314 / Failed: 0` and the overall run concluded success. Reconciliation: 2 tests failed on FIRST attempt and passed on RETRY — the reporter appears to reflect first-attempt outcomes; the pipeline summary reflects final outcomes. Neither is necessarily wrong, but neither DECLARES which it reports, so the same run carries two contradictory statuses with nothing explaining the divergence, and a consumer cannot tell whether it is reading a retry-inclusive or retry-exclusive result. Same family as TD-UI-030 (reporter reports a zero-spec run as PASSED) — the reporter's status semantics are undeclared. | Medium | Design question, not a patch: does run status mean "did every test pass on first attempt" or "did every test eventually pass," and must a run carrying flakes be able to report PASSED at all without surfacing the retry count in the status itself? Evidence: CI 29658308368. Flaky tests were cart.spec.ts TC052 and healing-verification.spec.ts HV006 — neither in the files this milestone changed. Related: TD-UI-030 (reporter status honesty). |
| TD-147 | **Enrich the identity probe to record the navigation chain (initial landing origin, redirect hops, final origin) rather than a single post-navigation page.url().** Nova Option B: a CAPABILITY milestone that would make the baseUrl observation genuinely DISCRIMINATIVE (ADR-019 axis 2) rather than gated to inconclusive. With the full chain an SSO/proxy redirect is distinguishable from an actual base-URL change, so baseUrl could again yield a definitive divergence conclusion when the evidence supports it. | Medium · NB | **Explicit acceptance bar: must clear its own evaluation harness with real verification before it ships — must NOT be smuggled into an honesty fix.** Until then the ADR-019 axis-2 gate reports inconclusive honestly, a declared limitation not a defect (hence · NB). Successor to TD-146. **Until TD-147 lands, baseUrl cannot report divergence AT ALL — it can only confirm a match or decline to conclude; TD-147 is what earns the signal's positive conclusion back. Its practical value is HIGHER than · NB implies: the tag reflects only that no false positive remains, not that the signal is fully useful.**  **RESTATED (2026-07-19, TD-148): the comparison this served was RETIRED.** The original purpose — make baseUrl DISCRIMINATIVE for identity divergence — no longer exists (no comparison to serve). What survives is OBSERVATION FIDELITY: record the login-surface navigation chain (initial landing origin, redirect hops, final origin) as a richer OBSERVATION on the login-surface surface, NOT for any comparison. Today landing-url records only the final page.url(); the hop chain is more informative diagnostic context. No conclusion is drawn from it. |
| TD-148 | **Focused axis-2 audit of detector -> comparison boundaries that produce USER-VISIBLE conclusions (ADR-019 discriminative competence).** Per Nova's revised posture: NOT a repository-wide sweep, NOT purely on-contact. Scope is exactly the surfaces where a comparison output reaches a user as a decision: appType, crawl-strategy selection, identity divergence (remaining signals), triage verdicts, heal classification. One question per surface: could the same observation support materially different conclusions? OUT of scope: parsers, helpers, any comparison whose output is not user-visible. Follows architectural risk, not implementation breadth. | High | **Rationale (load-bearing): the posture changed on DISCOVERABILITY, not recurrence.** TD-146 survived implementation, review, a clarifying comment, acceptance, and CI; only the dedicated audit caught it. When a defect class is invisible to the people closest to it, on-contact detection is not a real control. Find-only when run — flag with evidence, decide with Raj. Related: ADR-019 (axis 2), TD-146. **Onboard slice COMPLETE (2026-07-19) — findings TD-149 (authType, High), TD-150 (appType), TD-151 (baseUrl). Remaining TD-148 surfaces still UN-audited: crawl strategy selection, triage verdicts, heal classification, appType wherever else it is compared.** **UPDATE (2026-07-19): the Onboard slice was resolved by RETIREMENT, not fix — TD-149/150/151 closed by retiring the identity-divergence comparison (observation domain != conclusion domain) and replacing it with an observation-only surface. The remaining four surfaces stay un-audited and must use the domain-first sequence (domain -> representation -> discrimination).** **UPDATE (2026-07-20): ALL FIVE SURFACES NOW AUDITED (remaining-surfaces slice, against 9fd3d27). Results: crawl strategy selection — UNDER-DETERMINED (ADR-019 axis 2) + confidence-asserted-beyond-evidence (ADR-015/018) → TD-156 (High); triage verdicts — CLEAN; heal classification — CLEAN (two noted non-TD-148 caveats); appType internal dispatch (`=== 'rest-api'/'graphql-api'` routing) — NOT-USER-VISIBLE, out of scope; appType Onboard detection — UNDER-DETERMINED (ADR-019 axis 2) → TD-157 (Medium); Onboard identity divergence — resolved by capability retirement (the 2026-07-19 slice, TD-149/150/151). What CLEAN establishes: triage and heal were ASKED the three questions (Q1 domain / Q2 representation / Q3 discrimination) + aggregate + defaults and PASS — it is NOT a warrant they are free of OTHER defect classes. Two heal caveats recorded as noted-NOT-failures: (i) HealReporter status reads "No healing required" whenever healsSucceeded===0 (HealReporter.ts:85), which can absorb an attempted-and-failed heal in the report wording — mitigated because the authoritative failure grade (could-not-verify) is preserved at the result layer; (ii) heal discrimination ultimately depends on the CALLER's assertion strength (a weak assertion could accept a wrong-but-passing element), upstream of the classifier. TD-148 audit COMPLETE.** |
| TD-152 | **SPA-aware settling policy for the login-surface observation probe.** observeLoginSurface (LoginSurfaceObservation.ts) observes password-field/app-shape via detectAuthType/detectAppType with the DEFAULT immediate-count settling (DomcontentloadedPolicy), NOT the SPA-aware policy Bootstrap.detect() uses — so an SPA login form rendered after domcontentloaded reads 0 (TD-110). Switch the observation probe to the SPA-aware settling policy. | Medium | HOLD RELEASED by both reviewers: with NO conclusion drawn from the observation (TD-148 retirement), better observation fidelity is a straight improvement and cannot inflate confidence. Must carry its OWN settling blind spot in the mechanism text (a form rendered after the policy still reads 0 here). Separate change, not the TD-148 retirement commit. Related: TD-110, TD-149 (the false positive this fidelity gap fed, now moot post-retirement). |
| TD-153 | **Role reported `authOutcome: "succeeded"` with NO credentials supplied for that role in the run — an outcome-layer honesty INVESTIGATION (do NOT patch on contact).** Observed during Raj's manual verification of `fb320c1`, scenario 3 (SauceDemo: `LOCKED_USER_CREDENTIALS` set to `locked_out_user:secret_sauce`; `STANDARD_USER_CREDENTIALS` explicitly UNSET for that run). Resulting model (`models/saucedemo/app-model.json`, generatedAt `2026-07-20T15:51`, run superseded by the 15:5x scenario-3 run): `standardUser` — credentialsEnvKey `STANDARD_USER_CREDENTIALS` unset this run, `authOutcome: "succeeded"`; `lockedUser` — credentials supplied, `authOutcome: "failed"`; `guestPage` — authFlow none, `authOutcome: "succeeded"`. Downstream effect: `pagesDiscovered` 1 → 5 and `evidenceState` "crawled-empty" → "crawled", BOTH on the back of standardUser's reported success. Compare scenario 1 of the SAME session, where standardUser had credentials supplied (deliberately wrong) and correctly reported `authOutcome: "failed"`. **TWO COMPETING EXPLANATIONS — do NOT assume either cause: (a) Cached auth-state reuse.** `.auth/standardUser.json` may have persisted from an earlier successful run; the one-time `.auth` clear happened before all three scenarios, so a success INSIDE the session could have rewritten it. If so, the honesty question is whether `authOutcome: "succeeded"` should distinguish AUTHENTICATED-THIS-RUN from REUSED-A-CACHED-SESSION — a reused session is not evidence the current credentials work, and collapsing both into one outcome is an ADR-019 discriminative-competence question at the OUTCOME layer. **(b) `authOutcome` defaulting to 'succeeded' in the absence of evidence.** If a role with no credentials reaches "succeeded" without an authentication actually being performed and verified, that is an outcome field defaulting to success with ZERO evidence — a direct ADR-015 violation, the most serious defect class in the codebase. **Scope note:** NOT a defect in the TD-148 login-surface observation surface — that surface behaved correctly across all three scenarios; this is an adjacent finding in the auth-outcome layer surfaced by the same run. | High | Investigation before fix, per standing rules. Priority High and NOT · NB: under (b) this is an outcome-defaults-to-success defect in a field that GATES `pagesDiscovered` and `evidenceState` — a false success that propagates into the model's own evidence claims; under (a) it is still an honesty gap at the outcome layer. Neither reading is cosmetic. Design conversation required if it turns out to be (b). Found during Raj's manual verification of `fb320c1`, 2026-07-20. Related: ADR-015 (assert only what evidence supports), ADR-019 (discriminative competence at the outcome layer), TD-148 (adjacent surface, behaved correctly). **RESOLVED 2026-07-20 — NOT A DEFECT (verdict (d)/OTHER). Both (a) and (b) refuted with evidence.** (1) **Single write path:** the model role `authOutcome` has ONE writer, `Crawler.ts:489` ← `roleAuthOutcomes` ← `Crawler.ts:176-180`; for a form-login role `'succeeded'` requires `authenticated===true`, produced only by `AuthManager.ts:139` after a real URL-changing login. No default/fallback/catch/coercion yields `'succeeded'` for form-login (refutes (b) structurally). (2) **No cached-reuse path exists:** `AuthManager` creates `browser.newContext()` with NO `storageState` (`AuthManager.ts:38`); repo-wide `storageState:` reader grep = 0 hits — `.auth` is write-only, never read back, so every form-login `'succeeded'` is a live this-run auth (refutes (a)'s mechanism). (3) **Mechanism named:** `npm run onboard -- --app=saucedemo` takes the Internal Fixture Mode branch (`cli.ts:144-167` → `new Crawler(config)` at `cli.ts:165`, root `.auth` + root `models/`) — the exact path that wrote the forensic root `.auth/standardUser.json` (real `standard_user` cookie, mtime 15:55:56Z, in the 15:5x window) and the root model. This path loads **NO dotenv** (no `--env-file`/`-r dotenv/config`/`NODE_OPTIONS`/`.npmrc`/tsconfig preload; no `dotenv.config()` in cli.ts/Crawler/Bootstrap) — so `.env` line 14 (`STANDARD_USER_CREDENTIALS=standard_user:secret_sauce`) is NOT the source on this path. The only source is the **shell process environment**: the operator's `unset` did not reach the crawl process (separate PowerShell session/window, or a persisted User/Machine-level var), so the run genuinely had valid credentials and standardUser authenticated for real. (4) **Settling test run 2026-07-20** (direct-Crawler path, `STANDARD_USER_CREDENTIALS` verifiably absent — presence-checked `false` at crawl start): standardUser → `authOutcome=failed, reachable=0, observedPostAuthUrl=(none)`, no `.auth` written — exactly the predicted credential-less shape, deterministically NOT `'succeeded'`. `'succeeded'`/`pagesDiscovered 5`/`evidenceState crawled` were all factually true and evidence-backed. Two honesty gaps that made a correct system LOOK like a defect spun off, ledger-only: TD-154 (credential provenance opacity), TD-155 (guest authOutcome conflation). |
| TD-154 | **Credential provenance opacity: the model records `credentialsEnvKey` but NOT whether — or from where — the credential resolved at run time.** A role carries `credentialsEnvKey: STANDARD_USER_CREDENTIALS`, but nothing in the model records that the key actually resolved to a value this run, nor its source (shell env vs `.env`-via-dotenv on the pipeline paths vs forge-ui `ExecutionContext` materialization vs a stored reference). An operator who believes they unset a variable cannot tell from the model that the run in fact had credentials and authenticated for real. **This is the exact honesty gap that made a correct system (TD-153) look like a default-to-success defect** and cost an investigation to rule out. Observation-fidelity item at the auth-outcome layer: the model should let a reader distinguish "authenticated with a credential resolved this run (source X)" from "no credential resolved." | Medium | Spun off from the TD-153 investigation, 2026-07-20 (verdict: NOT a defect — this opacity was the confusion's root). Design, not a patch: what provenance is honest to record without leaking secret values (presence + source-class, never the value). Related: ADR-013 (credential resolution policy), ADR-015 (assert only what evidence supports), TD-153 (the investigation this opacity triggered). |
| TD-155 | **Guest `authOutcome: 'succeeded'` conflation: `authFlow === 'none'` writes `'succeeded'` unconditionally (`Crawler.ts:177`).** A role that performed ZERO authentication reports the SAME outcome token as a role that authenticated against real credentials and was verified via a URL change. By design ("no auth needed" → succeeded), but at the outcome layer it collapses two materially different states — "authenticated successfully" and "no auth required" — into one value, so a consumer of `authOutcome` cannot distinguish them. ADR-019 discriminative-competence question at the OUTCOME layer (sibling to TD-153's (a) framing). | Medium | Spun off from the TD-153 investigation, 2026-07-20. Not urgent and not a false success (guest genuinely needs no auth), but the token conflation is a real discrimination gap — e.g. a distinct value such as `'no-auth-required'` vs `'succeeded'`. Design call for Nova. Related: ADR-019 (discriminative competence), TD-153 (surfaced by the same investigation). |
| TD-156 | **Crawl strategy is surfaced at hard-coded HIGH confidence from a single-page, single-timepoint observation that cannot support it — TWO defects, one row, because (2) makes (1) dangerous.** **(1) UNDER-DETERMINED (ADR-019 axis 2).** StrategyDetector observes the DOM shape of ONE page at ONE timepoint (domcontentloaded + a fixed 2s wait, `StrategyDetector.ts:66-176`), and Bootstrap runs it against the PRE-AUTH base-URL landing page (`Bootstrap.ts:296,304`). Materially different realities collapse to the same observed value: a genuinely sparse page, a login page, an SPA not finished hydrating by the 2s wait (the TD-110/TD-152 blind spot), and a failed or partial load ALL read `realLinks=0 / jsClickables=0` and route to the `bfs` "safe default" (`StrategyDetector.ts:162-163`). The code documents the gap itself: OrangeHRM, a real SPA, reads `isSpa:false` "by design" for lack of framework fingerprints (`StrategyDetector.ts:39-44`). **(2) CONFIDENCE ASSERTED BEYOND EVIDENCE (ADR-015, ADR-018).** `detectCrawlStrategy` stamps `confidence: 'high'` UNCONDITIONALLY (`Bootstrap.ts:157`), regardless of what was observed — so a `bfs` chosen precisely because the page yielded nothing (zero links, zero clickables, no evidence at all) is surfaced to the user as a HIGH-confidence strategy. An observation that discriminated nothing produces the system's most confident output; the composed output is stronger than its weakest constituent (ADR-018), and a confidence value defaults to a confident literal with no evidence backing it (ADR-015). **User-visible:** Onboard Detection `crawlStrategy` + confidence (`projects.ts`), and the Crawl-tab strategy label "Link Following"/"Click Discovery"/"Hybrid" (`crawl.ts:35-40,122,174`; `CrawlPage.tsx:176-181`). **Contrast that isolates (2):** appType (TD-157) hits the SAME hydration blind spot but defaults honestly to confidence 'low' with source 'default-fallback' (`Bootstrap.ts:207`) — same observation weakness, opposite honesty outcome; (2) is independent of the domain question and is the more serious of the two. | High | Found by the TD-148 remaining-surfaces audit, 2026-07-20, against `9fd3d27`. NOT · NB — a live over-confident claim in a user-visible field, not a declared limitation. **Design conversation required (Nova). Do NOT patch — and specifically do NOT fix by simply lowering the confidence literal: the question is what evidence warrants what confidence, not which constant to substitute.** Same shape as the retired TD-150 (single-page, single-timepoint pre-auth-landing observation surfaced as a whole-application conclusion; SPA-hydration blind spot the sharpest competing cause). Remediation blocked pending a Nova ruling on whether TD-156/TD-157 are two signal-level fixes or one observation-context fix, exactly as TD-148 was. Related: ADR-019 (axis 2), ADR-015/018, TD-110/TD-152 (hydration blind spot), TD-150 (same shape, retired), TD-157 (sibling appType finding), TD-148 (the audit). **RULING (2026-07-20, Nova + Finn, Aiden-arbitrated, Raj-confirmed): ONE root cause (the observation context is too weak to characterize the whole application), TWO remedies.** Crawl strategy is an OPERATIONAL DECISION the pipeline cannot proceed without — Option 0 (delete the claim, as identity-divergence was retired) does NOT generalize here. Strategy KEEPS its decision (choosing `bfs` because evidence is weak is ACCEPTABLE per Nova and Finn) and LOSES its false confidence (the unconditional `'high'` at `Bootstrap.ts:157`). The quality limitation stays; the lie goes. Remedy = evidence-derived confidence, governed by the new architectural rule TD-158. **Do NOT fix by simply lowering the confidence literal** — the question is what evidence warrants what confidence. Design-first done this pass; implementation next pass. |
| TD-157 | **appType detection surfaced from a single PRE-AUTH landing-page observation (UNDER-DETERMINED, ADR-019 axis 2).** `detectAppType` (`Bootstrap.ts:192-208,306`) classifies the PRE-AUTH landing page via framework markers (`#root`/`#app`/`[ng-version]`/`[data-reactroot]`, framework `<script>` counts), falling back to links/forms counts. Surfaced as detected appType with confidence in Onboard Detection (`projects.ts:158,242`; `api/types.ts:24`). A genuine MPA, an SPA not hydrated at count time (spaDom AND spaScript both 0 — TD-110 blind spot), a static login shell in front of an SPA, and a sparse page all collapse to the `desktop-web → web-ui` default (`Bootstrap.ts:203-207`). **Q1 proxy caveat:** for a gated app the page classified is the login shell, not the application — the same door/room shape as the retired TD-150 identity-divergence signal; the framework-signal path is a defensible proxy (an SPA typically serves the same bundle pre- and post-auth), which is why this is Medium rather than High. **Mitigating and recorded honestly:** the fallback defaults to confidence 'low' with source 'default-fallback' (`Bootstrap.ts:207`), so the under-determination does NOT compound into an over-confident claim — that single difference is why this is contained where the strategy finding (TD-156) is not. | Medium | Found by the TD-148 remaining-surfaces audit, 2026-07-20, against `9fd3d27`. **Design conversation required. Do NOT patch.** Same shape as the retired TD-150 (single-page, single-timepoint pre-auth-landing observation surfaced as a whole-application conclusion; SPA-hydration blind spot the sharpest competing cause). Scope note: appType internal dispatch (`appType === 'rest-api'/'graphql-api'` routing in Crawler/FlowDetector/generators) is NOT-USER-VISIBLE and out of TD-148 scope; this row covers ONLY the user-visible Onboard-detection surfacing. Remediation blocked pending a Nova ruling on whether TD-156/TD-157 are two signal-level fixes or one observation-context fix, exactly as TD-148 was. Related: ADR-019 (axis 2), TD-110 (hydration blind spot), TD-150 (same shape, retired), TD-156 (sibling strategy finding, High), TD-148 (the audit). **RULING (2026-07-20, Nova + Finn, Aiden-arbitrated, Raj-confirmed): SAME root cause as TD-156, DIFFERENT remedy.** appType detection is an EPISTEMIC CLAIM ("this application is X"), not an operational decision — but Option 0 still does not apply (the pipeline needs an appType to route). The honest `'low'` + `source: 'default-fallback'` on the absent branch (`Bootstrap.ts:207`) is the CORRECT existing behavior and STAYS — it is the reference for what the present branches should become. The remedy is evidence-derived confidence on the PRESENT branches (`'high'` at `:198`, `'medium'` at `:204`), governed by TD-158 — NOT deletion, NOT a literal swap. Design-first this pass; implementation next pass. |
| TD-158 | **Evidence-derived confidence as an architectural rule: confidence must be DERIVED from evidence, never assigned by implementation.** Root cause shared by TD-156/TD-157 and surfaced across the Part-2 confidence audit (2026-07-20, against `9fd3d27`): multiple detectors stamp a constant `confidence` literal that asserts more than the observation supports. The rule has three load-bearing parts. **(1) Asymmetry (Aiden):** finding a positive signal (a framework marker, a password field) is real positive evidence; NOT finding one is absence of evidence, NOT evidence of absence — the two branches must NEVER produce symmetric confidence (this asymmetry is exactly how OrangeHRM, a genuine SPA, reads non-SPA). **(2) Single-pre-auth-timepoint ceiling (Aiden):** even WITH a positive signal present, one page at one moment pre-auth cannot warrant `'high'`; `'high'` asserts cross-sample/cross-state certainty a single sample does not provide. The honest ceiling for a single pre-auth sample is `'medium'`. **(3) Auditable, not asserted:** each confidence should carry a `source` distinguishing evidence-matched from default-fallback, and a machine-readable `reason` naming the evidence (mirroring the login-surface `mechanism`/blind-spot pattern), so the value can be audited rather than trusted. | High | Found by the TD-156/157 design pass + the Part-2 confidence audit, 2026-07-20. **Per Nova this is an explicit architectural rule or an ADR-015 CLARIFICATION, not an implementation guideline.** Part 2 shows the rule spans MULTIPLE subsystems — the Bootstrap detector cluster (`crawlStrategy :157`, `authType :178/:179`, `appType :198/:204`) AND the ModuleClassifier keyword table (`ModuleClassifier.ts:33-41`) both stamp LITERAL-UNEARNED confidence — so an ADR is likely warranted rather than a local clarification; **the ADR-vs-clarification call is Nova's, now that the Part-2 count is known.** Governs the TD-156/TD-157 remedies. Contrast the CLEAN derived producers (`deriveHealConfidence`, `computeConfidence`, `deriveFlowConfidence`, model-provided triage confidence) as the target pattern. Related: ADR-015 (assert only what evidence supports), ADR-018 (aggregate to the weakest truth), ADR-016 (carry the evidence), TD-156, TD-157, TD-159 (the positive pattern). |
| TD-159 | **Positive design heuristic (extracted from the two CLEAN TD-148 surfaces): every detector should either VERIFY DIRECTLY, or PRESERVE UNCERTAINTY EXPLICITLY.** Captured per Nova because the ledger records almost exclusively what went wrong, and the two things that went right are more useful as a template than any defect. **Preserve-uncertainty reference — TRIAGE:** `insufficient-evidence` is a first-class representable verdict, and ambiguous evidence routes there rather than collapsing into a concrete category (`ai-triage.ts`; input-health cap TD-067; weakest-truth default). **Verify-directly reference — HEAL:** it re-runs the caller's REAL assertion against the healed target rather than trusting that a selector resolved (`SmartLocator.ts:80-84`, TD-065; confidence bounded to the weakest constituent by `deriveHealConfidence`). A detector that does NEITHER — draws a conclusion it cannot verify and cannot honestly hedge — is the TD-156/TD-157 defect class. | Low | Design heuristic for future detectors, logged 2026-07-20 from the TD-148 remaining-surfaces audit. Not a defect and not actionable on its own — a reference to cite when designing or reviewing any new detector/confidence surface. These are the reference implementations for detector design. Related: TD-148 (the audit that surfaced them), TD-158 (the rule they exemplify), ADR-015/016/018. |
| TD-160 | **A test suite codified the confidence defect and hardened it against review.** `verify-td110.test.ts` asserted the over-graded confidence values ADR-020 later declared defective — `form-login → 'high'` (T6/T7) and, most sharply, `authType 'none' (an ABSENCE) → 'medium'` (T8), whose comment *"absence stays weaker evidence than presence"* explicitly defended grading an absence as merely weak rather than as no evidence (the exact OrangeHRM asymmetry, ADR-020 §2). The suite survived audit and review alongside the Bootstrap literals it was testing. **Recorded NOT as an open defect** — the assertions are corrected in the TD-158/ADR-020 commit (form-login → `medium`, absence → `low`/`default-fallback`, T8's comment rewritten to state the §2 rule). It is logged as a **pattern worth remembering**: a test that asserts a specific wrong VALUE makes the wrong value load-bearing and hardens it against exactly the review that should catch it — a `deepEqual` on `'high'` reads as a passing test, not as a codified lie. (Also affected, same class: `verify-td108.test.ts` T6/T7 and `verify-td112-td122.test.ts` T5 asserted the ModuleClassifier literal `'high'`.) | Low | Logged 2026-07-20 from the TD-158/ADR-020 implementation. **Consider, when convenient, whether other suites assert VALUES rather than PROPERTIES in the same way** — a `deepEqual` on a graded literal is the smell; asserting *"confidence is a function of the observation"* (source/reason present, found ≠ not-found) is the property that would have failed loudly when the value was wrong. Not urgent; a review-hygiene note, not a defect. Related: TD-158 / ADR-020 (the rule), TD-156/TD-157 (the literals these tests protected), ADR-011 (verify before assert). |
| TD-161 | **ModuleClassifier grants `'high'` on a 2+ keyword-hit match, while every other single-observation producer caps at `'medium'` under ADR-020 §4 — is that ceiling earned?** Lexical URL-word matching is a DISTINCT observation method, so ADR-020 §3 permits it a distinct ceiling. But §3 also holds that a method earns a higher ceiling only BECAUSE it observes more — and two corroborating keyword tokens **from the same URL string** is thin grounds for the system's TOP grade. The derivation is honest either way (source + reason travel with it, and the two-token vs one-token distinction is real); the open question is the CEILING, not the mechanism. Concretely: `/web/index.php/auth/login` earns `'high'` from `auth`+`login` — two tokens, but one observation of one string. Contrast `authType`/`appType`, where even a positive marker caps at `'medium'` because a single pre-auth sample cannot establish a durable characteristic (§4). | Low | Recorded 2026-07-20 from the TD-158/ADR-020 implementation — **for review, not rework.** Revisit when ADR-020 ceilings are next examined; consider whether `'high'` should require corroboration from a source OTHER than the same URL string (e.g. page content, or agreement across pages), consistent with §3's "earns more because it observes more." Not urgent and not a defect — the current grade is honest; this asks whether the ceiling is calibrated. Related: ADR-020 §3/§4 (ceilings are a property of the observation method; a single sample cannot buy high), TD-158 (the rule), TD-159 (verify-directly / preserve-uncertainty heuristic). |

This is the **first live confirmation** of the TD-158/ADR-020 work: a genuine no-login page graded at the FLOOR with `default-fallback` (not the old over-graded `'medium'`), carrying its own §2 reason. **Recorded deliberately as a positive result** — the ledger records almost exclusively defects; a verified fix is worth the same visibility. Note also (cross-cutting with TD-162/TD-163): the ADR-020 provenance `reason` strings are what made the TD-162/TD-163 measurement defects DETECTABLE at all — without the printed counts, the cross-detector contradiction would not have been visible from the output. | Low · NB | Not a defect and not actionable — a verified-positive record from the 2026-07-20 manual Onboard verification at `b421a2d` (saucedemo.com, opensource-demo.orangehrmlive.com, wikipedia.org, standalone CLI crawl path). | TD-166 | **`authType.value` is NON-DETERMINISTIC — two onboarding runs of the SAME application can persist different configurations.** authType has FOUR production paths: static `detectAuthType` (`Bootstrap.ts:197-217`), agent VALUE correction `applyAuthTypeObservation` (`:308-313`, called `:629`), agent GRADE upgrade `upgradeField(authType,'high')` (`:640`), and an agent reason-note (`:643`). Paths 2-4 live in `runAgentPhase`, which is **skipped on `--dry-run`** (`:572`) and otherwise depends on goal synthesis, agent execution, credentials, network, timing, and AI budget. The consequence reaches the VALUE, not just the grade: for any app where static detection reads `'none'` — precisely the SPA-login case (TD-110 blind spot: the form has not rendered at the pre-auth count) — the persisted value is `'none'` if the agent does not correct it and `'form-login'` if it does. **So the same application, onboarded twice, can yield two different configs, determined by whether an agent happened to succeed rather than by anything about the application.** SauceDemo is stable (password field present pre-auth → static reaches `form-login` deterministically; only the grade varies). OrangeHRM is not. `appType`/`crawlStrategy`/`appName`/`baseUrl` are single-path (static); only authType is multi-path. | High | **NOT · NB — a live product defect (config non-determinism), not a note.** Surfaced via the ground-truth-fixture design (the fixture-flakiness it was found through is the SYMPTOM; this is the disease, and more serious). **Investigation before fix.** The design question is NOT "how does the harness avoid it" (settled by the static-only harness ruling) but **whether a non-deterministic correction path should persist a config value at all, and if so how the persisted value declares which path produced it.** Found 2026-07-21. Related: TD-110 (the SPA pre-auth blind spot the 'none' path rides), TD-167 (loginUrl coupled to the same static/agent split), ADR-020 (the grade layer, correct; this is the value layer), the `signals?`/ground-truth work that surfaced it. **⚠️ BOUNDED-THRESHOLD MITIGATION shipped (auth-settling package) — TD-166 STAYS OPEN; the multi-writer ownership defect is UNRESOLVED and explicitly EXCLUDED from any completion claim.** What shipped: the `SPA_AUTH_SETTLING_POLICY` observation ceiling raised 3s → 10s (invocation is once per onboarding — verified, so no per-page multiplication); the auth-surface observation is recorded (`authSurfaceObservationMs` / `Ceiling` / `Threshold` / `ExceededExpectation` in `authType.signals`, on the manifest AND the TD-168 detection log line); the 3s expectation is PROVISIONAL (the previous ceiling reused, NOT an evidence-derived performance standard); and the static floor is now `'unknown'`/`'unknown'` (never a false `'none'` — TD-173 asymmetry: a bounded non-observation is not proof of 'no auth'). `generateConfig` maps detection `'unknown'` → config `authFlow: 'none'` at BOTH sites (unauthenticated-crawl default unchanged; ConfigAdapter/AuthManager untouched). The `applyAuthTypeObservation` guard was fixed to correct from BOTH `'none'` and `'unknown'` (else the TD-110 agent-correction would regress) — pinned by test. **This narrows the TIMING window in which the static value floors low; it does NOT make the persisted value single-source.** CONTAINMENT (Nova): `verify-td166-auth-settling.test.ts` M7 deliberately demonstrates the divergence path STILL EXISTS (the same non-observation yields `'unknown'` statically vs `'form-login'` after agent correction) — if M7 ever asserts equality, the defect was silently closed. Also note: ADR-020 §5's low-vs-unknown distinction no longer applies to authType (a zero password-field count carries no information — TD-173), superseding test A4's prior stance. Shipped 2026-07-23. |

| TD-168 | **Pipeline logging does not meet the transparency standard; detection decisions are not logged at all.** Standing requirement (Raj, recorded): logs must be extremely transparent — a reader must be able to reconstruct the actual sequence of events from the log alone; a log line may not be vague. This is ADR-015 applied to logging: a log line is a claim about what happened and must carry its evidence. **Audit (sample of 6 subsystems, 2026-07-21):** **L1 — `Bootstrap.detect()` logs NOTHING** — appType, authType, crawlStrategy are decided and never logged (not the value, not the branch, not the signals); the single most important onboarding decision is invisible in the log, a reader must open the manifest. **Highest-value fix.** **L2 — `Crawler.ts:188`** `"Auth failed for <role> — skipping role"` — a conclusion without its inputs (the URL/error are on a different line; this line cannot be reconstructed alone). **L3 — `Crawler.ts:631`** authType-correction line omits the raw before/after and which agent goal reached the form. **L4 — `ai-triage.ts:227`** logs verdict + confidence; the reasoning is behind `--verbose` — a conclusion without its basis by default. **The good pattern exists:** `StrategyDetector.ts:168-173` logs the decision AND its raw signals (`"Mode: <mode> \| spa:<x> realLinks:<n> jsClickables:<n>"`) — it has simply never been applied to the detection or verdict layers. | High | **Rule to encode when designed:** every logged observation or decision names the METHOD that produced it, prints the RAW VALUES (not a characterization), and is reconstructable from that line alone. L1 directly complements the `signals?` work — the same structured signals feeding the ground-truth harness should feed a transparent detection log line. **Design conversation before implementation; likely an ADR given it spans every subsystem.** **COVERAGE CAVEAT:** the audit sampled 6 subsystems; `src/pipeline` beyond ai-triage, the forge-ui server, and the healing/verification loggers were NOT swept — this row is a starting point, not a complete finding. Found 2026-07-21. Related: ADR-015 (the honesty law), TD-166 (a decision non-determinism a transparent log would have exposed), `signals?` work. **⚠️ L1 RESOLVED `a675167`** (2026-07-23) — `Bootstrap.detect()` now logs one transparent line per detected field (authType / renderingModel / crawlStrategy / appName) via `formatDetectionLogLine`: value + confidence + source + raw signals (`={}` when absent, so a two-run log diff aligns column-wise) + reason (which carries the METHOD, JSON-quoted so spaces/commas never break the columns). Reconstructable from the line alone (Raj's rule); follows `StrategyDetector.ts:169`'s convention. Divergence: DetectedField has no discrete `.method` (it lives inside `.reason`), so `reason` is printed verbatim rather than parsed. The correction log (`Bootstrap.ts:656`, `authType corrected none → form-login`) is KEPT — a separate event. Verified with a real SauceDemo static-only run (actual log lines) + `verify-td168-detection-log.test.ts` (L1–L3, pure format). Line numbers in the finding above had drifted (TD-166/TD-168 joint audit, 2026-07-23) and "appType" is retired terminology (now renderingModel). **⚠️ L2 + L3 RESOLVED `55668eb` (2026-07-23; hash reconciled post-rebase — was `78efd18` before the rebase onto the CI run-history commit) — logging only, no behaviour/value change.** L2 (`Crawler.ts:188`): the auth-failed line now carries its inputs (role / authFlow / authenticated=false / startUrl), reconstructable alone; the root auth ERROR stays on the separate `[AuthManager]` producer. **L3 — the row's `Crawler.ts:631` attribution was WRONG:** there is no authType-correction line in `Crawler.ts` (`git log -S` confirms it never existed there); the correction lives in `Bootstrap.ts` (now ~`:724`), and TD-166 had made its message factually wrong — it hardcoded `'none → form-login'` when the post-TD-166 before-value is usually `'unknown'`. Fixed to log the raw before→after (value/confidence/source) + WHICH agent goal triggered it (via a read-only BEFORE snapshot) — the line that makes TD-166's multi-writer divergence diffable across two runs. **L4 = WORKING AS DESIGNED (no change):** the reasoning is NOT lost — it reaches TWO persisted artifacts (the DB `ai_triage.root_cause` via `ai-triage.ts:397`, and the triage report JSON/MD), so ADR-015's persist-the-basis requirement is met; `--verbose` (`ai-triage.ts:228`) gates ONLY the console echo of the multi-sentence reasoning — a display choice, not a transparency failure. Recorded WAD so it is not re-raised. A related DATA-layer defect spun off → **TD-179** (Bootstrap `:753`/`:771` hardcode `'none'` in persisted evidence/notes — the same post-TD-166 inaccuracy but in DATA not a log; excluded from L3's logging-only fix). **ROW STAYS OPEN on the COVERAGE SWEEP ONLY:** the 2026-07-21 audit sampled 6 subsystems; `src/pipeline` beyond ai-triage, the forge-ui server, and the healing/verification loggers were NEVER swept, so more L-items likely remain. Status: L1/L2/L3 resolved, L4 WAD — the FINDING is not complete until that sweep runs. |

Per-file at scan time: AI_ONBOARDING_CHECKLIST 29, DOCUMENTATION_INDEX 31, CODEX_ONBOARDING 11, AI_CONSTITUTION 4, AI_WORKFLOW 3. Full-tree scope (beyond these 5 docs) is UNMEASURED and larger. | Medium | **Candidate INPUT to the DETECT tier of the "Documentation Moves Forward With Code" DR (`DECISION_LOG`; commit `2282551` on origin) — NOT a hand-fix backlog row.** Durable fix = a CI reference-resolver that fails when a doc names a file/path unresolvable from the citing location; this 78-count is the concrete evidence motivating that tooling. A manual pass ("Commit 11") is possible but the fix is NON-uniform and awaits a Raj scope + style ruling: prose refs can become resolvable markdown links; code-fence refs (the reading-order / checklist fenced blocks) cannot be links and need full relative-path *text* (a styling change), and paths differ per citing dir so a blind find/replace would mint NEW broken links. Do NOT silently absorb into an unrelated commit (Standing Rule 8). Scan detail: `notes/review-scratch/bareref-sweep-count-2026-07-22.md` (ephemeral). Related: the reference-naming standing rule (`CLAUDE.md`, Commit 10), DR `2282551` (DETECT/DERIVE/GATE), TD-176 (sibling doc-drift defect). |

| TD-016 | `FlowDetector.mergeConfigSeeded()` only ever compiles login steps + one trailing `assert-navigation` from a `FlowHint` — it ignores the rest of the hint text. Concretely: `onboarding.saucedemo.config.ts`'s `checkout-happy-path` hint reads "Add item to cart, proceed through checkout to completion," but the compiled flow's steps are just login + assert-navigation to `inventory-html`; no add-to-cart or checkout steps are ever generated. Confirmed 0 of 3 flows in the current SauceDemo model touch `cart-html` at all. Found while investigating TD-013 — not bundled into that fix per the standing "flag scope expansion, don't silently absorb it" rule. **Update (TD-018 session):** now directly confirmed as a live blocker rather than a theoretical gap — with TD-018 fixed and generation finally succeeding, `checkout-happy-path.generated.spec.ts`'s actual compiled content is just login+assert, identical in shape to the inferred-flow files, which is what puts it in TD-017's collision path below. **Update (Part 1 of TD-016/017/019-Implementation-Brief.md, this session):** root cause confirmed and fixed at the source — `mergeConfigSeeded()` was deriving (wrong) login steps instead of parsing the hint's business intent at all; that derivation is now removed entirely (see TD-017/TD-019 updates below). This session's fix is a prerequisite for this entry's own ask, not an implementation of it — removing the wrong login-step derivation is what frees `mergeConfigSeeded()` to eventually parse the hint's real business-step content; that parsing itself was not built this session. This entry's own original ask — compile the hint's actual business steps (add-to-cart, checkout) — remains genuinely unresolved: `FlowHint` (`types.ts`) carries no structured step data beyond free-text `hint`, so config-seeded flows now compile with `steps: []` rather than wrong ones. Confirmed live: regenerated `checkout-happy-path.generated.spec.ts` is a valid, compiling, passing-shape smoke test (`expect(standardUser).not.toHaveURL(/error|404/)`), not the wrong login+assert it was before — but it's not the real checkout flow either. The free-text-parsing design decision this row already called for is still outstanding; not built per the brief's own explicit out-of-scope instruction. **Update (TD-045 investigation, this session):** confirmed live, via a fresh post-TD-031-fix SauceDemo crawl, exactly why navigation-edge compilation structurally can't recover this hint's "add item to cart" business step on its own: `AuthManager` performs login outside the page-visit loop (no edge ever recorded for it) and `MUTATING_TEXT_PATTERNS` deliberately excludes "Add to cart" from crawl-time clicking (to avoid polluting crawl state) — so `FlowDetector.enrichWithAi()`'s edge-grounding (TD-031) will *always* fall back to an ungrounded `assert-navigation` (correctly flagged via `groundingWarnings`, not silently) for these two specific transitions, on any app, regardless of how well TD-031's fix works otherwise. This isn't a defect in TD-031 — it's live confirmation of this row's own point: the AI-proposed/navigation-edge paths are structurally edge-blind to business intent like "add to cart," which is exactly why TD-016's free-text-hint-parsing (or a structured `FlowHint` step list) is the only path that can actually compile a working add-to-cart flow. | Medium | Needs a design decision: either parse intent out of free-text hints (fragile, likely AI-based — would mirror `FlowDetector.enrichWithAi()`'s existing pattern), or replace/extend `FlowHint` with a structured step list so config authors can declare the full flow, not just the login boilerplate. Affects flow quality on every app that uses `flows:` hints, not just SauceDemo.  **REFRAMED 2026-07-18:** the originally-described defect (login+1-assert compile ignoring hint text) no longer exists — mergeConfigSeeded now deliberately compiles ZERO steps with honestly-derived confidence ("no steps rather than wrong steps"). The remaining REAL gap: FlowHint text is never compiled into business steps (capability + design work). |
| TD-020 | `FixtureGenerator` emits the same unconditional successUrl `waitForURL` for every role regardless of whether that role's flow is a negative/blocked-login case. `lockedUser`'s fixture (`fixtures.generated.ts:37-46`) waits for `**/inventory.html**` after login, but `locked_out_user` is supposed to be rejected and stay on the login page with an error banner — it never reaches `inventory.html`, so the fixture itself times out before the test body ever runs. Found while triaging a CI "Stable suite" run (2026-06-19 20:08–20:21 UTC, `ci_remaining_failures.txt`): of 5 SauceDemo `generated`-project failures, 4 (`TC-GEN-001`–`004`, via `standardUser`) showed `page.fill` timeout on `[data-test="username"]` (TD-017's known cause), but the 5th (`TC-GEN-005`, `inferred-flow-lockedUser-*.generated.spec.ts`, via `lockedUser`) showed a distinct `TimeoutError: page.waitForURL: Timeout 15000ms exceeded ... waiting for navigation to "**/inventory.html**"` — confirmed by reading `fixtures.generated.ts` directly: both fixtures are identical in shape (login, then unconditional 15s `waitForURL` to the success URL), and `lockedUser`'s login is expected to fail by design. | Medium | Distinct from TD-015 (flaky `lockedUser` auth during crawl — different code path, `AuthManager`, not `FixtureGenerator`) and TD-017 (redundant fill in the test body — presupposes the fixture's own login succeeded; here the fixture setup itself fails, so the test body never executes). Needs a design decision: should `FixtureGenerator` distinguish "expected to succeed" roles from "expected to fail/reject" roles when emitting the post-login wait condition? Likely needs role config to declare expected outcome (success vs. rejection), not just `successUrl`. |
| TD-024 | TD-013's actual resolution (commit `9a0ec90`, `OnboardingConfig.pagePrerequisites` / `PageDefinition.prerequisites` / `Crawler.applyPagePrerequisites()`) is a manual, per-app config-hint mechanism — a human must already know a page is stateful and hand-author its prerequisite steps in `onboarding.{appName}.config.ts`. This is a different design than `docs/archive/TD-013-Implementation-Brief.md` specified: auto-tagging pages stateful/stateless during crawl via the `MUTATING_TEXT_PATTERNS` signal, sourcing prerequisites from `FlowDetector`'s flow data instead of a new config-hint structure, plus a REVIEW-tab editable override. The brief was drafted without knowledge that TD-013 had already shipped two days earlier (`9a0ec90`, 2026-06-18) — TD-014 wasn't resolved until 2026-06-20, after TD-013, despite the brief's "HOLD until TD-014 clears" framing. **This is not a defect** — the shipped fix is verified live and working: `reports/verify/saucedemo-verify-report.json` shows `setupFailures: []`, `elementsPassed`/`elementsTotal: 103/103` (including all 22 `cart-html` criticals), `confidenceLevel: "HIGH"`. The concern is scalability, not correctness: the manual-hint approach requires a human to notice and hand-author a hint for every stateful page on every newly onboarded app; nothing in the crawler currently infers statefulness on its own, so coverage silently depends on whoever writes the config remembering to add the hint. | Low · NB | Untriaged — whether building an auto-detection mechanism along the lines the brief proposed is worth the added design/implementation cost vs. continuing to rely on hand-authored hints has not been decided. No fix proposed. See `docs/archive/TD-013-Implementation-Brief.md`'s superseded notice for the full design comparison. |
| TD-039 | Newly exposed by the SPA-discovery-merge fix's own evidence-gathering (TD-021/026/027), not a regression it introduced: across two full, granularly-instrumented OrangeHRM crawls, fixed waits (the 8×250ms poll loop + up to 1200ms post-navigation reset, both pre-existing and untouched by the merge) account for ~73.5% of the discovery sweep — and one selector, `[class*="menu-item"]`, is responsible for the large majority of that on its own: 972 of ~2583 total matched elements across all 16 `NAV_SELECTORS` entries combined (every other selector: low double digits), ~56% of the entire discovery sweep by itself (`poll=354614ms(x972) reset=1657847ms(x939)` of a `discoverySweepMs=3561205` total). This was previously masked by the much larger per-selector page-reload cost the merge fix removed; with that gone, this broad, generic class-substring selector's sheer match volume is now the dominant remaining cost on OrangeHRM specifically. | Low→Medium · NB | Not investigated beyond the measurement — worth a future look at whether `[class*="menu-item"]` can be narrowed or deduplicated against elements other selectors (`.oxd-main-menu-item`, `nav a`, `[role="navigation"] a`) already cover, without touching the rest of `NAV_SELECTORS`. Explicitly out of scope for the merge fix itself, per its "no general performance tuning beyond removing the specific redundant wait" boundary. No fix proposed. |
| TD-036 | `SpecGenerator.generateFlowTests()`'s "full flow" test (`mainBody`, `SpecGenerator.ts:386-395`) replays every `assert-navigation` step via `emitStep()` (`:354-361`), which compiles to `await expect(${role}).toHaveURL(/${escaped}/)` with the pattern built from `urlPattern.replace(/\//g, '\\/').replace(/\./g, '\\.')`. For a flow's first step targeting the `home`/`/` page, the resulting pattern is the unanchored regex `/\//` — which matches any URL containing a slash, i.e. every URL the app will ever produce. The assertion can never fail regardless of actual page state, so it isn't really checking anything. Same root step as TD-034 (the pre-auth `home`/`/` page appearing in a flow's own step list) but not one of TD-034's counted failures, since this one passes vacuously instead of failing. Found while verifying the TD-034 fix — confirmed live in the regenerated `login-to-inventory.generated.spec.ts`: `await expect(standardUser).toHaveURL(/\//)` is the first line of the full-flow test, while the page is actually already on `/inventory.html`. | Low · NB | Not currently breaking anything — flagged as a correctness gap worth fixing whenever flow-replay assertions get revisited, possibly alongside TD-026/TD-031 (the same flow-step-replay machinery). No fix proposed. |
| TD-037 | **New latent gap introduced by the SPA-discovery-merge fix (item 1/2 of `SPA-Discovery-Merge-Implementation-Brief.md`), not pre-existing debt** — flagged at design time, before the fix landed. The merged per-URL loop in `SPAStrategy.crawl()` nests its discovery call inside the `if (visited.has(normalized)) continue` skip, so a frontier URL already present in `visited` *before* `crawl()` starts now gets no discovery pass at all (the original two-loop structure ran discovery against every frontier URL unconditionally, regardless of whether it had already been visited). Unreachable by SauceDemo or OrangeHRM today — `Crawler.ts` always seeds a fresh, empty `visited` Set per role for pure SPA-mode crawls (`:111`), so SPAStrategy's frontier can never start pre-seeded there. Reachable via `SelfCorrectionEngine`'s hybrid-escalation path: `HybridStrategy.crawl()` runs its SPA half (`SPAStrategy.crawl()`) with a `visited` Set already populated by its own BFS half, and that SPA sub-call's `startUrl` is the same URL BFS already started from — exactly the pre-seeded scenario this gap describes. | Low · NB | Currently unreachable by either reference app (Hybrid mode isn't the literal resolved `crawlMode` for either app's config today, though `SelfCorrectionEngine` can escalate into it). Re-check the moment Hybrid-mode work (item 4 of the same brief, currently on hold) actually starts — that's exactly the work that would make this reachable in practice. |
| TD-038 | **Pre-existing discovery-loop bug, not introduced by the SPA-discovery-merge fix** — `discoverViaSelectors()`'s `nav button` selector matches OrangeHRM's persistent "?" help button (present identically on every page sampled: dashboard, admin/viewSystemUsers, pim/viewEmployeeList, leave/viewLeaveList, leave/assignLeave). Clicking it (the click-fallback branch — the button has no `href`/`target` attribute, confirmed `href=null, target=null`) fires a JS `window.open()` that Chromium opens as a genuine new `Page` in the same `BrowserContext`, entirely outside `discoverViaSelectors()`'s/`discoverViaButtonText()`'s own `context.newPage()`/`page.close()` pairs — those pairs are individually correct (every `newPage()` has a matching `close()`), they just never know this *other* page exists. Confirmed live via `context.waitForEvent('page', ...)`: every click produced a real page that navigated to a page-contextual Zendesk help article (`starterhelp.orangehrm.com/hc/en-us/articles/...`, e.g. "How to Add a User Account" for `admin/viewSystemUsers`, "How to Filter the Employee List" for `pim/viewEmployeeList`) — matching exactly what was visually observed as unexplained open tabs during a live crawl. Reported by Raj from a screenshot showing tabs titled "How to Add a User," "How to Filter the Employee List," "How to Approve Leave." | Medium | Confirmed mechanism only — no fix implemented, per instruction (separate root cause and separate fix from TD-021/026/027, not folded into that work). Two candidate directions, not decided: (a) a generic `context.on('page', ...)` listener during the discovery click loop that closes any unexpected popup, app-agnostic and catches both literal `target="_blank"` hrefs and JS `window.open()` calls; (b) a cheap pre-click skip for elements with a literal `target="_blank"` attribute *when present*, which wouldn't have caught this specific button (no such attribute exists on it) but is a free win for any future app using literal blank-target links. Likely needs both, combined. Also flagged: these leaked popup pages, accumulating silently across a 30-page crawl, are a plausible contributor to the cross-run timing noise seen while verifying TD-021/026/027 (66m/159m/55m across three otherwise-identical runs) — not confirmed as the cause, just a plausible one worth keeping in mind if that noise is investigated further. |

| TD-047 | `VerificationRunner.run()` navigates to each page via a fresh `page.goto(targetUrl, { waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' })` reload before checking critical elements (`VerificationRunner.ts:209`) — this does not reproduce the actual navigation path real usage (and generated specs/fixtures) takes: login → in-app navigation → check, never a raw reload. Confirmed live, isolated to a single variable: SauceDemo's `inventory-sidebar-link` reports **visible** after a `goto()` reload but correctly **hidden** after the real login-flow path — same element, same page, same Playwright APIs (`isVisible()`, `waitFor`, `expect().toBeVisible()` all agreed both times) — only the navigation method differs. SauceDemo's burger-menu (react-burger-menu) only acquires its real hidden ARIA/CSS state through the SPA's own runtime; a fresh reload resets it to an initial state Playwright doesn't detect as hidden. | High | Discovered while attempting **TD-033**'s fix (see that entry — the `attached`→`visible` change was a confirmed no-op against this same element, for this exact reason). Means `VerificationRunner`'s HIGH-confidence certifications may be unreliable for any element whose true state depends on SPA-runtime/session state rather than raw DOM presence — broader and more fundamental than TD-033's original "attached vs visible" framing, which this entry supersedes in diagnosis (TD-033 stays open as the originating report). Real fix is likely changing `VerificationRunner`'s navigation pattern to traverse via the app's actual flow (or at least the fixture's login path) rather than a direct `goto()` reload per page — a structurally bigger change than TD-033's original ask, needs its own design conversation before any code. Related to **TD-013** (the other "`VerificationRunner` navigates with no real setup" issue — that one about missing prerequisite *state*, this one about the navigation *method* itself resetting SPA-runtime state). **Design decision (Option D, signed off by Raj):** explicitly decided NOT to change the navigation pattern (no in-app traversal, no login-path workaround — rejected for cost/complexity). Decided not to fix the underlying navigation gap; instead redefined what verification claims to guarantee, and added a report flag so the limitation is visible rather than implicit. Implemented: `ElementResult`/`FlowResult` (`VerificationRunner.ts`) now carry a `verificationTier` field (`'dom-presence'` \| `'http-status'` for elements, `'interaction'` for flows), plus contract comments at `verifyElement()`, `verifyFlow()`, and `buildReport()`'s `confidenceScore` computation explaining the blend. **Stop-condition finding surfaced during implementation, not assumed away:** flow verification (`verifyFlow()`/`executeStep()`) already performs real `click`/`fill`/`selectOption` interaction and a real `state: 'visible'` wait for `assert-element-visible` steps — a genuinely stronger, pre-existing mechanism distinct from element verification's DOM-presence-only check, and it feeds 40% of `confidenceScore`. Also found `ElementResult` itself isn't uniform either: REST/GraphQL apps populate it via HTTP-status checks (`'http-status'`), not DOM checks at all. Both are now represented honestly via `verificationTier` rather than collapsed into one inaccurate label. **Verified live:** SauceDemo's `inventory-html:inventorySidebarLink` now reports `status: "passed", verificationTier: "dom-presence"` — the pass is unchanged (per design, check criteria isn't changing), but it's no longer presented as an unqualified signal. |
| TD-048 | Found while spot-checking TD-032's heuristic expansion, explicitly out of scope for that fix — within-page structural repetition inflates critical-element counts on data-dense OrangeHRM pages independently of the cross-page shared-shell duplication TD-032 addressed. `pim/viewEmployeeList` and `admin/nationality` both hit the 100-element harvest cap (`ElementClassifier.harvestElements()`'s `elements.slice(0, 100)`), with many of those elements being per-row form inputs (e.g. repeated row-level checkboxes in an employee table) that Rule 1 (any form input regardless of type) correctly, individually marks critical — each one is a real, distinct, functioning input, not noise, but a table with N rows produces N near-identical critical entries the same way TD-032's cross-page nav-shell duplication did, just within one page instead of across many. | Low→Medium | Not investigated beyond the observation, per instruction. Related to TD-018's container/row disambiguation concept (`containerIndex`/`containerHint`), but a different mechanism — TD-018 is about *naming* repeated elements uniquely, this is about whether *counting every one of them as independently critical* is the right signal. No fix proposed. Also note: the 100-element harvest cap itself means very large tables (more than 100 interactive elements) are silently truncated before `determineCritical()` ever sees the rest — a separate, pre-existing limit, not changed here. |

Useful for re-triaging after an API outage or retrying a failed AI step without burning 30-40 min of CI time. | Low · NB | Backlog, no fix now. Job 2 already consumes the `forge-reports` artifact rather than re-running tests, so an independent trigger would mainly need a way to supply that artifact (or read the last run's `reports/` from cache/DB). |

| TD-058 | CI's `forge-framework.db` is ephemeral — gitignored, **not** in the `reports` cache, **not** in the `forge-reports` artifact, and recreated empty by `db:migrate` every run. | TD-060 | Split-brain source of truth for run data. `run-history.json` + `trends.json` are git-tracked, committed, and auto-committed back to main (`e2e-pipeline.yml` `Commit run history back to repo`, `:274-281`); `forge-framework.db` (`RunsTable`) is gitignored/ephemeral. This is the mechanism behind the observed divergence (local DB had 14 runs while tracked `run-history.json` had 2): both are written together in `appendToHistory()` for new runIds, but the gitignored DB accumulates locally while the tracked JSON is reset by checkout/pull/CI auto-commits. | Medium | Needs an explicit decision: pick ONE authoritative store. | TD-064 | **[CRITICAL — unearned confidence]** GENERATE has **no behavioral validity self-check** before a generated spec is emitted/reported — only format guards (uniqueness `PomGenerator.assertUniqueNames`; role-shape `SpecGenerator.assertValidRoleStrategy:293`). A generated flow "smoke test" asserts only `await expect(role).not.toHaveURL(/error|404/)` (`SpecGenerator.ts:380`), and navigation steps compile to `toHaveURL(/…/)` (`:360`, incl. TD-036's vacuous `/\//`). So a **green** generated flow test (e.g. checkout-happy-path) implies the flow works when it verifies almost nothing, and specs with wrong click targets / non-unique selectors (TD-016/030/050) are presented as trustworthy artifacts with no signal they may be invalid. | High — acute CRITICAL (spec overclaiming) RESOLVED via honest omission (FC catalogue closed); residual = design-stage assertion-strength mechanism (R2), OPEN | Design decision needed: a generate-time validity check (meaningful-assertion / resolvable-unique-selector / reachable-target) or an explicit "unvalidated" marker on emitted specs. Do not fix — Aiden sign-off. **Fix approach (logged, the 39 TC-GEN failures made concrete):** generated specs assert poorly — most assert `toBeVisible()` on non-unique selectors → Playwright strict-mode violations; a few are wrong URL/flow assertions or logic errors (e.g. locked-user). Root cause is the SPEC GENERATOR emitting bad assertions, not the app and not triage — fix the generator (source) and regenerate, do NOT hand-edit 39 files. Agreed approach (next session, design-first, Nova in the loop): (1) AUDIT the generator root cause (read-only) + CATEGORIZE the 39 into distinct failure TYPES (e.g. A: non-unique-selector strict-mode [bulk]; B: wrong URL/flow assertion; C: locked-user logic; D: ambiguous/other — the data decides how many types exist); (2) move the 39 broken generated specs to a reference/temp folder (keep as before-evidence; do not delete); (3) redesign the generator scoped to the audited root cause (likely: unique-selector strategy + correct assertion templates — `.first()`/`.nth()`/`count` where matches are non-unique; right flow expectations); (4) regenerate ONE representative per failure TYPE (not arbitrary 2-3, not all 39) — each MUST PASS against the real app (saucedemo), and diff old-vs-new to confirm the root cause is gone (pass = gate, diff = explanation); (5) iterate until all representatives pass; (6) regenerate the FULL set → all pass → ONLY THEN push (nothing pushes until a fully working set exists). Success criterion is the TEST RESULT (new TC goes green against the app), not the diff. App-agnostic: fix the generator, not the symptom. **Catalogue structure (adopted from Nova's review):** reframe the 39 from individual tests into FAILURE CLASSES (FC-001..N); Step 1 of TD-064 = build the Failure Class Catalogue. Per-class template — `FC-XXX: <name>` · Frequency: `<n>/39` · Symptoms: `<observed failure signature>` · Root Cause: `<why it happens>` · Generator Defect: `<what capability the generator is missing>` · Proposed Fix: `<generator-level fix that eliminates the whole class>` · Representative: `<one TC-GEN id — becomes regression-suite seed>` · Success Criteria: regenerated representative passes consistently against the real app · Preventable: YES (generator defect — TD-064 targets these) / NO (outside generator control). TD-064 primarily targets Preventable=YES classes. Catalogue yields: prioritization (highest-frequency first), generator redesign requirements, regression-suite seeds (one rep per class), and MEMORY.md content. Nova review checkpoints: (1) Failure Class Catalogue, (2) Generator Architecture Changes, (3) Evidence Strategy. **TD-064 progress (Session 29):** Failure Class Catalogue built + 3-way approved (docs/td-064/). FC-001 (non-unique selector, 24) FIXED & proven live — cardinality evidence carried onto ElementDefinition (commits dabecf9/d75b648/d2706c9/e1ef3ee). FC-003 (state-gated visibility, 4) FIXED & proven live — observedState captured at crawl, generator downgrades hidden elements to toBeAttached() with annotation per ADR-011 (commits 9128d8e/3907035/6d1493d/442429a/fbc9bbe/e50e53b). Cumulative: 39 -> 15 -> 11 failures, 0 regressions. ADR-011 (verify-before-assert) created. Remaining: FC-002 (wrong nav/URL, 8 — next), FC-004a (reachability, ~2), FC-004b (~1, preserve as insufficient-evidence). Catalogue is a LIVING DOCUMENT — frequencies recalculated as masking clears. Latent TDs logged: time-based flow-spec IDs (orphan risk), dead tier3Assertions field. FC-002 (wrong nav/URL, 8) FIXED & proven live — grounding ('observed'|'inferred') on FlowStep; generator asserts toHaveURL only for observed nav, downgrades inferred to not.toHaveURL(/404|error/) else omits (commits af14f2e/ffbb2bc/844221d/cd10fe6). Cumulative: 39 -> 15 -> 11 -> 6, three preventable classes closed, 0 regressions. Re-audit reclassified the remaining 6 into FC-004a (5) + FC-004b (1). **FC CATALOGUE RESOLVED — re-synced 2026-07-22 from the catalogue + shipped code (this row had frozen at the FC-002-done/FC-004a-next snapshot).** FC-001/002/003/004a/004b are ALL closed. FC-004a resolved by HONEST OMISSION (the generator emits no assertion/interaction it cannot back with observed evidence) — on-origin `8f3e9ca` (Stage 1: critical-elements gated by nav-grounding) + `75aabd8` (Stage 2+3: grounding-aware click gate); FC-004b by omitting auth-failed roles. Code proof in current `SpecGenerator.ts`: reachability gate `:479`, grounding-gated click `:399`, `interaction-never-observed` `:387`, `prerequisite-unverified` `:379`, auth-failed-role omit `:56`. TC-GEN-001 was NOT an FC-004a defect — EJECTED to TD-080 (cross-app spec-glob contamination). Full per-class lineage: `docs/td-064/TD-064-Failure-Class-Catalogue.md` (⚠ that catalogue cites PRE-REBASE/orphaned hashes — verify by subject, not by hash; a reference-integrity follow-up, TD-177 family). **SCOPE SPLIT — "FC catalogue RESOLVED" = the generator STOPPED OVERCLAIMING (honest omission), NOT "TD-064 fully done":** the general **assertion-strength mechanism** (evidence-layer — every spec carries `none/smoke/shallow/meaningful` + `validity:unproven`, so a `meaningful`-claimed / existence-only spec is a detectable gap) is the REMAINING work (R2). It is design-stage and PARTIALLY present already (`src/evidence/types.ts:93` defaults `validity:'unproven'`; `SpecGenerator.ts:492` caps assertion-strength to arrival confidence), gated on the Evidence Layer design (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_TARGET_EVIDENCE_LAYER.md` §3 Generate). Also still out-of-scope under TD-064: positive cart/checkout coverage (gated on TD-013) and negative-path lockout modeling (deferred). Ground truth re-derived 2026-07-22, `notes/review-scratch/td-064-fc-004a-ground-truth-2026-07-22.md`. |
