| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-014 | `SPAStrategy.crawl()` only discovered one hop from the start page — visited discovered URLs once, never recursed into links found on those pages. **Root-cause correction (important — the original diagnosis was wrong):** TD-014 was originally opened on the claim that OrangeHRM's three pages (`recruitment/viewCandidates`, `pim/viewPersonalDetails/empNumber/7`, `performance/searchEvaluatePerformanceReview`) required 2+ hops to discover. Two clean post-fix runs (`logs/td014-orangehrm-after.log`, `logs/td014-orangehrm-rerun2.log`) both found all three of these at **depth 1**, not depth 2 — they were never genuinely second-hop pages; the original 18–21-page count variance was a depth-1 discovery-timing/hydration artifact (OrangeHRM's flyout submenus intermittently not fully expanded during the original one-hop crawl), exactly as flagged as a possibility in `MEMORY.md` (`project_spastrategy_one_hop_limitation.md`). This does **not** mean the fix was unnecessary: both clean runs' depth-2 sweep independently found **9 genuinely new pages** only reachable via real recursion into a depth-1 page — `admin/nationality`, `admin/addTheme`, `pim/addEmployee`, `pim/viewDefinedPredefinedReports`, `leave/applyLeave`, `leave/viewMyLeaveList`, `leave/assignLeave`, `recruitment/viewJobVacancy`, `pim/contactDetails/empNumber/7` — none of which are reachable from the dashboard sidebar directly. That is real, structural one-hop coverage loss, independent of the three originally-named pages. | `97783c2` | Implemented per the signed-off brief (`TD-014-Implementation-Brief.md`): replaced `SPAStrategy`'s single discovery pass with a frontier queue keyed by depth (mirrors `BFSStrategy`'s existing shape), discovery (no AI) run across a full depth's frontier before any classification at the next depth, and `OnboardingConfig.budgets.maxDepth` — already typed and already set per-app, but previously read only by `BFSStrategy` — now also honored by `SPAStrategy`. Practical effect: OrangeHRM's already-set `maxDepth: 3` was silently inert until now; this fix makes it take effect (crawl now reaches depth 2 before exhausting the discovered frontier, within a `maxDepth: 3` budget). `onboarding.orangehrm.config.ts`'s `aiCalls` budget raised 30→100 to support the larger element-naming volume the now-functioning depth-2 recursion produces — required for the fix to run to completion within budget, not a separate tuning decision. Added a surfaced `aiBudgetStatus: 'within-budget' \| 'degraded'` field on `AppModel.app` (`types.ts`), set by `Crawler.ts` and `ApiSpecCrawler.ts`, with a banner-style console log at crawl completion — verified both states live: `logs/td014-orangehrm-after.log`/`logs/td014-orangehrm-rerun2.log` show `WITHIN BUDGET` for OrangeHRM's real depth-3 crawl, `logs/td014-orangehrm-degraded-test.log` shows `DEGRADED` under an artificially starved budget (pages=4, depth=1, ai=1). SauceDemo confirmed unaffected (`logs/td014-saucedemo-after.log`: 8 pages, reached depth 1 of maxDepth 5 — a shallow app, no second-hop pages exist to find, behavior unchanged). Restful Booker confirmed unaffected (`logs/td014-restfulbooker-after.log`: `ApiSpecCrawler` path only touched by the additive `aiBudgetStatus` field, ​8 endpoints, no `SPAStrategy` code in its path). Full OrangeHRM run reaching depth 2 across 30 pages with ~100 AI naming calls took 57m45s end-to-end (`crawlDurationMs: 3465461`, `models/orangehrm/app-model.json`) — logged separately as TD-021 (crawl-time cost at higher `maxDepth`, untriaged, not fixed here). Evidence logs kept in the repo under `logs/td014-*.log` rather than cleaned up, since they're the only record of the corrected root-cause framing above. |

## Resolved (Session 12 — TD-022 re-heal history fix)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-022 | `SmartLocator.resolve()` deleted a stored heal entry (`HealStore.retireHeal()`, a hard `delete`, no tombstone) the moment it stopped resolving, *before* the fallback-chain/Vision re-heal that follows in the same call had a chance to run `recordHeal()` against it — wiping `firstHealed`/`consecutiveSuccesses` on every re-heal of a previously-healed element instead of carrying them forward, even though `recordHeal()`'s own carry-forward logic (`existing?.firstHealed ?? event.timestamp`, `(existing?.consecutiveSuccesses ?? 0) + 1`) was written correctly and just never got a live entry to read. Distinct from the mechanism diagnosed in this entry's prior update (`HV002`'s own direct `healStore.retireHeal()` test-setup call, unrelated to this code path) — that mechanism is real but is the test's own intentional determinism setup, not a bug; this is the separate, genuine production-path bug the prior update flagged as unconfirmed ("whether those paths reproduce the same loss on real, non-test heal entries remains unconfirmed"). | `1cc5fb2` | Per `TD-022-Implementation-Brief.md`: removed the `healStore.retireHeal(this.def.key)` call from `SmartLocator.resolve()`'s "stored selector no longer works" branch (was line 32), replacing it with a `console.log` describing what's actually happening ("no longer resolves -- re-deriving via fallback/vision") so the user-facing signal isn't silently dropped — distinct wording from the genuine retirement log still emitted by the untouched primary-recovered branch a few lines below. `HealStore.retireHeal()` itself and that primary-recovered call site were not touched. Verified via a new natural-code-path repro (`scripts/verify-td022.ts`, not part of the permanent suite) that seeds real multi-call heal history via `recordHeal()` (not the contrived retire-then-recordHeal test pattern) then drives the literal `resolve()` method: **Scenario A** (stored heal fails -> strategy-chain re-heal) — pre-fix `logs/td022-before-fix.log` shows `firstHealed` reset from seeded `2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z` to the live run timestamp and `consecutiveSuccesses` reset 2->1; post-fix `logs/td022-after-fix.log` shows `firstHealed` correctly held at `2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z` and `consecutiveSuccesses` correctly incremented 2->3. **Scenario B** (stored heal fails -> Vision re-heal, `VisionHealer.prototype.heal` deterministically stubbed to avoid a real API call/budget spend while still exercising the real `resolve()` vision branch) — same pattern, `firstHealed` held at seeded `2026-02-01T00:00:00.000Z`, `consecutiveSuccesses` correctly incremented 3->4, confirming the vision-heal path carries forward identically to the fallback-chain path. **Scenario C** (primary selector recovers while a stale heal entry exists) — confirms the untouched primary-recovered branch still deletes the entry (`getEntry()` returns `undefined` after `resolve()`), i.e. no regression on the one case where deletion is correct. `npm run check`: zero errors. Re-ran `healing-verification.spec.ts` (`logs/td022-healing-verification-spec.log`): 14/14 passed (chromium + webkit), including HV002 unchanged — confirmed by direct trace that HV002's own `healStore.retireHeal('test.loginButtonHeal')` setup call runs *before* `resolve()` is invoked, so `storedSelector` is already `undefined` by the time `resolve()` executes and the removed call site was never reachable in that test to begin with; HV002 was not relying on the old destructive behavior, and its intent was not changed. One pre-existing, unrelated flake observed: HV003 timed out once on webkit (30s test timeout) before passing on retry — that test makes a real, no-stub Claude Vision API call on `about:blank` and is the kind of network-latency-driven webkit timing flake already logged under TD-LOW-002/Session 9's notes, not a behavioral change from this fix (the fix only replaces an in-memory `delete` + a `console.log` call, both negligible relative to network latency). Also ran `edgeCases.spec.ts`'s EC011 self-healing test: 2/2 passed (chromium + webkit). Out of scope per the brief, not investigated: the orthogonal in-memory-singleton-store concurrency note (theoretical race on two concurrent `resolve()` calls for the same key) — no evidence found that it's a live risk, so left unlogged as instructed. |

## Resolved (Session 13 — TD-016/017/019 Part 1: stop re-deriving login)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-019 | OrangeHRM's `admin-login` flow (`models/orangehrm/app-model.json`) compiled a `click` step against `web-index-php-maintenance-viewMaintenanceModule:confirm` — a "confirm" button on the **Maintenance module** page — instead of the actual login submit button on `auth/login`. Root cause: the real login page is structurally never in `this.pages` at all — `AuthManager.authenticate()` logs in entirely outside the crawl loop, via its own short-lived `page` instance, before any `PageVisitor.visit()` call — so `FlowDetector.mergeConfigSeeded()`'s `this.pages.find(p => p.isAuthPage)` was guaranteed to either find nothing or coincidentally match an unrelated password-gated page. Confirmed live it matched OrangeHRM's Maintenance-module re-auth prompt because that happened to be the first of 11 such false-positive `isAuthPage` pages in crawl order — not crawl non-determinism, as originally suspected when this entry was opened. | `d6f7550` | Per `TD-016-017-019-RoleStrategy-Implementation-Brief.md` Part 1: removed all login-step derivation from `FlowDetector.mergeConfigSeeded()`/`identifyCandidates()` — flows now assume the role's fixture/`AuthManager` equivalent has already authenticated, so there is no wrong page/element left to resolve a login click against. Verified live via `scripts/verify-td016-017-019-part1.ts` (no live re-crawl; recomputes flows against the already-crawled model/pages on disk): `admin-login`'s recomputed flow is `steps: []`, no longer referencing `viewMaintenanceModule:confirm` at all — committed in `models/orangehrm/app-model.json`. **This resolves only the wrong-button/wrong-authPage half of the original combined TD-019 entry.** The second, originally-stacked half — the malformed `role`-type strategy value that this wrong click step happened to also expose (`button[name='Confirm']` rendered as an invalid `[role="..."]` CSS string) — is a separate, unrelated defect (strategy value shape, not flow/page resolution) and is **not fixed by this change**; tracked as its own entry, **TD-029**, pointing to Part 2 of the same brief (not started). End-to-end `npm run onboard:generate -- --app=orangehrm` could not be re-verified this session — blocked by the unrelated TD-025 (duplicate element names abort generation before flow/strategy code is ever reached). |

## Resolved (Session 14 — TD-025 + TD-029: OrangeHRM generation blockers)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-025 | OrangeHRM's `web-index-php-recruitment-viewRecruitmentModule` page (100 elements) had 2 surviving name collisions post-`ElementClassifier.deduplicateNames()` — `recruitmentActionButton12`/`13` each appeared on 2 elements, aborting `PomGenerator.assertUniqueNames()` and blocking all OrangeHRM generation. Root cause (confirmed via `TD-025-TD-029-Implementation-Brief.md` investigation): not a deeper case of TD-018's tier-3 collision pattern — tiers 1-3 all still work correctly for their intended cases, confirmed live (a genuine two-series collision on the same `containerIndex` is still correctly split by tier 3). The actual gap is structural: no part of the pipeline ever had a whole-page view of final names. `resolveResidualCollisions()` only ever sees indices within one `baseName` group; a singleton the AI named uniquely at naming time (skipped by `deduplicateNames()`'s `if (indices.length < 2) continue`) was never recorded anywhere for cross-checking against other groups' independently-computed names. Confirmed mechanism: one member of each colliding pair was a tier-2-computed name (`recruitmentActionButton1` + containerIndex `2` → `recruitmentActionButton12`, no separator); the other was a singleton the AI directly and uniquely named `"recruitmentActionButton12"` in a different naming batch, never flagged as a duplicate because it was never part of a multi-element group. | `41e03f6` | Added `ElementClassifier.enforceWholePageUniqueness()`, called once after tiers 1-3 (`deduplicateNames()`) run unchanged in `classifyPage()`. Walks the complete final-name list for every element on the page regardless of which tier (or no tier) produced it, and appends a running-counter `_2`/`_3`/... suffix to any name still colliding at that point — agnostic to *why* the collision happened, a pure global safety net rather than a patch for the specific 1-vs-12 pattern. Rebuilds `id` (`{pageId}:{name}`) for renamed elements, same invariant tiers 1-3 already keep. Verified live: directly replayed the exact `recruitmentActionButton12`/`13` collision from the pre-fix OrangeHRM model (100-element `viewRecruitmentModule` page, backed up before re-crawling) through the real `enforceWholePageUniqueness()` method — before: 2 names × 2 elements each duplicated; after: 0 duplicates, second occurrences correctly renamed to `recruitmentActionButton12_2`/`13_2` with `id` rebuilt to match and `disambiguatedFrom` recorded. Also re-crawled OrangeHRM fresh end-to-end (30 pages, 1051 elements) and confirmed zero duplicate names/ids across the whole model programmatically (not just the one known pair) — the live demo site's recruitment-module data has since changed (different candidate/job counts day to day), so the *literal* 100-element/12-vs-13 collision no longer reproduces from a fresh crawl; the direct replay against the originally-captured data is the controlling evidence for that specific pair. Re-crawled SauceDemo too (132 elements, 4 pages) and confirmed zero duplicates there as well — no regression. `npm run onboard:generate -- --app=orangehrm` completed for all 30 pages without `PomGenerator.assertUniqueNames()` aborting. `npm run check`: zero errors. |
| TD-029 | Role-type selector strategies could be populated with a malformed compound value instead of a bare ARIA role token — `ElementClassifier.buildRoleSelector()` constructed `${role}[name='${accessibleName}']` (e.g. `button[name='Confirm']`) as a single string. `VerificationRunner.strategyToSelector()` and `SpecGenerator.bestSelector()` both double-wrapped that string as `` [role="${value}"] ``, producing invalid CSS / a TypeScript syntax error; `EmitHelper.ts`'s `case 'role'` passed the value through unwrapped, silently producing a semantically-wrong selector (`button[name='Confirm']` parsed as the HTML `name` *attribute*, not the accessible name); `SmartLocator.buildLocator()` had no type-based branching at all. Confirmed at scale: 1023/1023 OrangeHRM role-type strategies were primary, 507 of those compound-valued; SauceDemo had the identical defect in 46/56 role strategies, masked because `data-test` priority wins there. | `dd6b186` | Per `TD-025-TD-029-Implementation-Brief.md` Part 2: split the role strategy into two real fields — added `accessibleName?: string` to both `SelectorStrategy` (`healing/types.ts`) and `Strategy` (`onboarding/types.ts`, plus `app-model.schema.json`); `value`/`selector` now carries the bare role token only. `ElementClassifier.buildRoleSelector()` returns `{ role, accessibleName? }` and `buildStrategyChain()` populates both fields instead of concatenating. `SmartLocator.buildLocator()` now branches on `strategy.name === 'role'` and calls `page.getByRole(selector, accessibleName ? { name: accessibleName } : undefined)` — new logic, no prior branching existed. `VerificationRunner.strategyToSelector()` and `EmitHelper.ts`'s `case 'role'` build Playwright's `role=` selector-engine string (the string-selector equivalent of `getByRole`) from the two clean fields, each with a validation guard that throws loudly if a role value is still compound. `SpecGenerator` was restructured further than the original brief's literal text, confirmed against the user mid-implementation: `bestSelector()` keeps a guarded `role=`-string fallback, but `emitStep()` and the critical-element-visibility loop now branch on strategy type via a new `locatorExprFor()` and emit a literal `${role}.getByRole(...)` call in generated spec text for role-type primaries, since generated specs are human-reviewed output where idiomatic Playwright code matters — confirmed via a synthetic flow built against the real fixed model: generated text reads `await adminUser.getByRole("button", { name: "Confirm" }).click()`, not a CSS string. `PomGenerator.generateSmartProp()` also needed updating (necessary wiring to keep the two fields intact through codegen, not separately named in the brief) to emit the bare role token + `accessibleName` field in the SmartLocator strategies array instead of routing role-type strategies through the CSS-selector-string helper. Verified live: fresh OrangeHRM crawl (1051 elements) shows 0 compound role values (down from 518 pre-fix), 685 with the new `accessibleName` field populated; the `confirm` element (`button[name='Confirm']` pre-fix) now stores `{ type: 'role', value: 'button', accessibleName: 'Confirm' }`. Fresh SauceDemo crawl confirms the previously-latent 46/56 compound strategies are also fixed (e.g. `inventorySidebarLink`: `link[name='All Items']` → `{ value: 'link', accessibleName: 'All Items' }`), with no dedup regression. Live `onboard:verify` run against OrangeHRM: 35/35 role-type element checks resolved directly via `getByRole` on the first try (no healing needed); the 2 `confirm` occurrences fell through to the css fallback and healed successfully — confirmed via a direct live Playwright check against the real app that `getByRole('button', {name:'Confirm'})` correctly finds the real button outside the verification run, so this reflects the page's pre-existing TD-019-documented re-auth-gating behavior, not a `getByRole` defect — and the fallback-then-heal itself is exactly the "role-type primary that fails should still fall through to fallback strategies" behavior the brief required to keep working. Deliberately triggered the validation guard in all three guarded functions (`SpecGenerator.bestSelector()`, `EmitHelper.strategyToSelector()`, `VerificationRunner.strategyToSelector()`) with a synthetic compound value — all three threw the expected loud error. `npm run check`: zero errors. **Found and logged separately, not fixed inline:** regenerating SauceDemo exposed a brand-new `inferred-flow-*` spec referencing stale/mismatched `pageId`s, breaking that one generated file's import — unrelated to the role-strategy fix; tracked as **TD-030**. |

## Resolved (Session 15 — TD-035 enforceWholePageUniqueness fix)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-035 | Regression inside TD-025's own fix — `ElementClassifier.enforceWholePageUniqueness()` doesn't cross-check its generated suffix against names tier-1/2's coincidental fallback (`disambiguateCoincidental()`) already finalized, so it can independently compute the same suffix (e.g. `_2`) for two unrelated collision groups. Confirmed live on OrangeHRM's `viewCandidates` page: duplicate name `candidateRowActionButton11_2`, which fatally aborted `PomGenerator` and blocked all OrangeHRM generation in the full validation run (16 of 30 page objects written before abort). | `1a44639` | Root cause investigated before any fix: `enforceWholePageUniqueness()`'s input (the full `elements` array) is already complete — it sees every tier-1/2/3 rename, since `applyRenames()` mutates elements in place before this method runs. The actual defect is that it generates its suffix via blind `${original}_${occurrence}` concatenation without ever checking whether that computed *string* already belongs to some unrelated element. Traced the real colliding pair via `disambiguatedFrom` breadcrumbs in `logs/validation-run-2026-06-21/generated-diffs/app-models.diff`: one half came from `disambiguateCoincidental()` (tier 1/2's non-structural fallback, **not** `resolveResidualCollisions()`/tier 3 as first suspected — corrected during investigation) producing `candidateRowActionButton11_2` for a 2-member coincidental group *during* `deduplicateNames()`; the other half was an unrelated, untouched element independently named `candidateRowActionButton11` by a different group's tier-2 output, which `enforceWholePageUniqueness()` then "fixed" by blindly suffixing to the same already-taken `_2` string. Fix: seed a `usedNames` set from the complete current name list; when generating a suffix, probe upward (`_2`, `_3`, ...) until landing on a string not already in that set, then register it immediately so later iterations in the same loop see it too. Scoped to `enforceWholePageUniqueness()` only — it's the designated last-line whole-page safety net, so fixing it alone closes the gap regardless of which earlier tier produced the conflicting name. **Verified live:** fresh OrangeHRM crawl (`logs/td035-orangehrm-recrawl.log`; 30 pages, 947 elements, within AI budget) followed by `npm run onboard:generate -- --app=orangehrm` (`logs/td035-orangehrm-generate.log`) completed **all 30 page objects** without `PomGenerator.assertUniqueNames()` aborting (previously stopped at 16/30). Programmatic check (`scripts/check-duplicate-names.ts orangehrm`) confirms **zero duplicate names/ids across all 30 pages / 947 elements** — not a spot-check. SauceDemo re-crawled in parallel to confirm no regression (`logs/td035-saucedemo-recrawl.log`): 4 pages / 132 elements, zero duplicates (matches TD-025's prior baseline exactly), generation succeeded (4/4 page objects). `npm run check`: zero errors. **On reproducing the exact original collision:** OrangeHRM's live demo recruitment-module data changes day to day (the same caveat TD-025 itself hit) — this fresh crawl's `viewCandidates` page (29 elements) didn't happen to reproduce the action-button-table density that produced the original 100-element collision. Directly replayed the actual captured pre-fix state instead (`scripts/verify-td035.ts`), reconstructing the exact three-element scenario from the diff's `disambiguatedFrom` breadcrumbs: before the fix this input would produce two elements both named `candidateRowActionButton11_2`; with the fix, the second collision correctly skips the already-taken `_2` and lands on `candidateRowActionButton11_3` — confirmed via direct script run, zero duplicates. |

## Resolved (Session 16 — TD-034 critical-elements reachability fix)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-034 | `SpecGenerator.generateFlowTests()`'s critical-elements loop (`SpecGenerator.ts:398-424`) emitted a "critical elements visible on X" check for every `assert-navigation` step in a flow's own step list, including pages the role's fixture has already passed through before the test body runs. Root cause: `SpecGenerator`'s constructor (`SpecGenerator.ts:21`) only takes `AppModel`, never `OnboardingConfig` or `FixtureGenerator`'s output — it has zero awareness of where a role's fixture actually lands. Confirmed: `login-to-inventory`/`complete-purchase-flow`'s first flow step targets `home`, which is the literal SauceDemo login page (`isAuthPage: true`) — a state-graph artifact (every navigation-edge flow starts at the crawl's pre-auth entry node, same family as TD-026/TD-031) — while `standardUser`'s fixture (`FixtureGenerator.ts:174-185`) already authenticates and waits for `successUrl` before handing control to the test body. Confirmed live: TC-GEN-004 and #015 (2 of 19 failures in the full validation run). | `0324e5c` | Fix: added a guard in the critical-elements loop using `RoleDefinition.reachablePageIds` (`types.ts:201`) — an already-existing, already-correct, already-populated model field computed directly from each role's own crawl session (`Crawler.ts:214-230`). If a step's target page isn't in the role's `reachablePageIds`, skip generating the check rather than guessing a replacement page. No new model fields, no `OnboardingConfig` coupling. `mainBody`'s `toHaveURL(/\//)` replay was deliberately left untouched, per instruction — logged separately as **TD-036**, since it passes vacuously rather than failing. **Verified live:** regenerated SauceDemo specs (`npm run onboard:generate -- --app=saucedemo`) — `login-to-inventory.generated.spec.ts` no longer emits "critical elements visible on home" (only "...on inventory-html" remains). The literal `complete-purchase-flow` flow id from the original report no longer exists in the current live crawl's flow set (live-site flow naming/composition has shifted since, same caveat already noted for TD-025/TD-035) — its cart-reaching functionality is now split across `add-to-cart` and `direct-cart-access`, both confirmed fixed the same way. `inventory-html`/`cart-html` checks for `standardUser` confirmed still emitted correctly across all four affected flows (`login-to-inventory`, `view-product-details`, `add-to-cart`, `direct-cart-access`) — no over-correction. **Side effect surfaced, not introduced by this fix:** the same guard also suppresses `locked-user-login`'s "critical elements visible on home" check for `lockedUser`, because `lockedUser.reachablePageIds` is `[]` — a consequence of TD-015's already-logged flaky-crawl issue (that role's crawl session recorded zero pages), not because `home` is genuinely unreachable for a rejected-login role. No live behavior changes as a result: `lockedUser`'s fixture already times out at `waitForURL` (TD-020) before the test body would run regardless, so that test was already non-functional before this fix. Flagging for whenever TD-015/TD-020 get addressed, since fixing either may require revisiting this guard for `lockedUser` specifically. Could not exercise the "target page genuinely IS reachable" branch end-to-end against a live flow in this model (no current flow has `guestPage` targeting `home`), but confirmed the underlying signal is correct at the role level: `guestPage.reachablePageIds` already contains `"home"`, so the guard would not suppress that case if it existed. `npm run check`: zero errors (after excluding one freshly-regenerated, never-previously-committed `inferred-flow-standardUser-*.generated.spec.ts` file affected by the separate, already-logged **TD-030** — same precedent as the TD-025/TD-029 session: don't bundle an unrelated defect's fresh evidence into this fix). Live run of all 6 affected SauceDemo generated spec files: zero occurrences of TD-034's failure signature (login-container/username/password visibility) anywhere in the output; the 11 failures that do occur all map to already-logged, unrelated tech debt — `toHaveURL` sequence failures with no click steps between assertions (**TD-031**), `inventory-sidebar-link` hidden in the burger menu (**TD-032**/**TD-033**), and `lockedUser`'s fixture timeout (**TD-020**). |

## Resolved (Session 17 — SPA discovery merge: TD-021/TD-026/TD-027 SPA half)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-021 | `SPAStrategy`'s discovery sweep accounted for 94.1% of OrangeHRM crawl time (`logs/td021-orangehrm-timing.log`), driven by the repeated full-page-load-per-selector pattern (`context.newPage()` + `page.goto()` + a 2000ms fixed wait, ×16 selectors, against every URL in the frontier). | `9ced2ec` | Per `SPA-Discovery-Merge-Implementation-Brief.md`: merged classify-then-discover into one page instance per frontier URL (`PageVisitor.visitKeepOpen()`, new — splits `visit()`'s classify logic out so `SPAStrategy` can keep the page open across both steps; `visit()` itself is an unchanged thin wrapper, so `BFSStrategy.ts` needed no changes). Eliminates 17 of 18 page loads per URL. Removed the now-redundant per-selector 2000ms wait in `discoverViaSelectors()`; all other pre-existing waits (8×250ms poll loop, 1200ms/1000ms post-navigation reset, button-text's 800ms/600ms) left untouched, per the brief's explicit "no general performance tuning" boundary. **Verified, with an honest result, not an optimistic one:** removing the redundant page-reload was the correct target — confirmed via two separate full, granularly-instrumented OrangeHRM crawls that the new per-match attribute-reading added for TD-026 (`matchClickedElement()`) is negligible overhead (2.4% and 2.0% of the discovery sweep, 36.6ms and 27.7ms average per call across 1987 and 2583 calls respectively) and not itself a bottleneck — an earlier anomalous run (159m26s, vs. baseline ~66m) that briefly suggested otherwise did not reproduce on retest and is attributed to OrangeHRM's live public demo site's own variability, not this fix. But the *overall* speedup is modest, not dramatic: five full OrangeHRM runs with the fix landed at 66m21s, 159m26s (anomalous), 55m02s, and 65m22s — overlapping the pre-fix baseline (66m27s–66m45s) more than clearly beating it, given high run-to-run site-side variance. The wait-time breakdown explains why: fixed waits (pre-existing, untouched here) are 73.5% of the discovery sweep in the cleanest measured run, and one selector — `[class*="menu-item"]` — drives the large majority of that on its own by sheer match volume (972 of ~2583 total matches across all 16 selectors). That cost was always there; removing the larger page-reload cost just stopped it from being masked. Logged separately as **TD-039**, explicitly out of scope here. SauceDemo and OrangeHRM page counts confirmed not reduced by the merge (OrangeHRM: 30 pages both before and after, same depth reached; SauceDemo: 9 pages post-fix vs. 8 pre-fix in an earlier comparison run — a real site-content difference, not a measurement artifact, not a regression). `npm run check`: zero errors. |
| TD-026 | `Crawler.ts` hardcoded every `StateEdge.trigger` to the literal string `'navigation'` instead of the id of the element that actually triggered the navigation, for both BFS and SPA modes. | `9ced2ec` | SPA half resolved as part of the same merge (BFS-mode click-based discovery never identifies a triggering element at all, so TD-026 was always SPA-only in practice). New `SPAStrategy.matchClickedElement()` reads the clicked element's own live attributes (data-test → id → role+accessibleName → text, mirroring `ElementClassifier.buildStrategyChain()`'s own priority order, `ElementClassifier.ts:347-376`) and matches them against the page's already-classified `ElementDefinition.strategies` — applied uniformly to both the href-read and click-fallback branches, since which branch triggered a navigation doesn't determine whether the triggering element can be identified. Falls back to the literal selector string used to find the element (never `'navigation'`) when no classified counterpart exists. **Verified live:** regenerated SauceDemo's `inferred-flow-standardUser-*` flow shows real `elementId` values — `inventory-html:item4ImgLink`, `inventory-html:item0ImgLink`, etc. — sourced from real `.inventory_item_img a` `NAV_SELECTORS` matches, not the literal `'navigation'` placeholder. (That same flow's `pageId`/`targetPageId` use short-form ids `"inventory"`/`"inventory-item"` rather than the canonical `inventory-html`/`inventory-item-html` — this is the pre-existing, already-logged **TD-030**, unrelated to and not touched by this fix.) |
| TD-027 | `Crawler.ts`'s `stateEdges` were built from consecutive pairs in `Array.from(visited)` (insertion/visit order) rather than an actually-recorded relationship — BFS half resolved separately in `90c4b6b`/`5b4858a` (real edges from `PageVisitor.extractLinks()`'s `outboundUrls`). SPA half remained open: SPA's click-based discovery never attached a relationship to what it found, so there was no equivalent signal to thread through. | `9ced2ec` | SPA half now resolved by the same merge that fixed TD-026 — `SPAStrategy.discoveredEdges` (a new public readonly property, populated during the merged classify-then-discover pass) carries real `{fromUrl, toUrl, trigger}` relationships, exposed to `Crawler.ts` without changing `SPAStrategy.crawl()`'s return type (so `HybridStrategy.ts`, which also calls `crawl()` internally, needed zero changes). `buildRoleStateEdges()` gained a `spa` branch consuming `discoveredEdges`, filtered to targets that were actually visited — `bfs` and everything else (including `hybrid`) are untouched. **Verified live, against the standard this session has held BFS to — proving the fix changes real behavior, not just confirming an already-correct case:** the same regenerated SauceDemo `inferred-flow-standardUser-*` flow shows all 6 of its product-image-link click steps correctly sourced from `inventory` (the real common parent page) — under the old visit-order pairing, these 6 sibling pages (all discovered independently from `inventory.html`, not from each other) would have been wired as a sequential chain (`item4→item0→item1→...`), exactly the sibling-miswiring pattern TD-027 describes. TD-027 is now **fully resolved, both halves**. TD-026 (trigger identity) is also resolved for both modes that produce a meaningful signal (SPA via this fix; BFS structurally never identifies a triggering element, only a link — that gap is inherent to BFS's link-following discovery method, not a remaining defect). Hybrid mode's edge construction remains on visit-order, unchanged, per the brief's item-4 hold — see **TD-037** for a related gap flagged during that design pass, and **TD-038** for an unrelated, pre-existing discovery-loop bug found incidentally while verifying this fix (untracked popup tabs from an OrangeHRM help button — separate root cause, separate fix, not folded in here). |

## Resolved (Session 18 — TD-031 edge-grounded steps, TD-040 roleId guard, TD-041 token truncation)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-031 | `FlowDetector.enrichWithAi()` compiled every AI-proposed (inferred) flow as a chain of `assert-navigation` checks with zero click/action steps between them — the AI was never asked for actions, only a page-ID sequence (`pageSequence`), and nothing downstream ever grounded that sequence against the real, crawled navigation graph. Confirmed root cause of 9 of 19 SauceDemo generated-test failures in an earlier validation run. | `ceaa338` | Real-data investigation first (25 real page-transitions checked across both reference apps): 0/25 needed multi-hop pathing (every gap was a hard dead end), 9/25 (36%) had no real edge at all (the dominant failure mode, not an edge case), and the AI hallucinated a `roleId` not in its own "Known roles" list (see TD-040). Design (signed off): AI stays edge-blind, keeps proposing page sequences only — no edge data added to the prompt. The **compiler** now grounds each consecutive pair against this role's real `stateGraph.edges` (`groupEdgesByRole()`, shared with `identifyCandidates()`'s existing per-role partitioning — never a different role's edges). Direct edge found → a real `click` step using the edge's real `trigger` as `elementId`. No direct edge → falls back to the pre-existing `assert-navigation`/`null` shape, but the flow now carries a `groundingWarnings` array (`FlowDefinition`, `types.ts`) naming which pair(s) fell back, and each one is logged — never a silent fallback. No multi-hop pathfinding built, per the measured 0/25 finding. **Found and fixed inline, scoped to this lookup only:** `this.urlToPageId()` strips `.html` entirely instead of converting it to `-html`, disagreeing with the real `PageDefinition.id` (the same TD-030 root cause, e.g. `"inventory"` vs the real `"inventory-html"`) — silently broke every edge lookup, since the AI's `pageSequence` uses the real page ids. Added `resolveRealPageId()` (resolves via `this.pages[].urlPattern` instead), used only inside this new grounding logic; `identifyCandidates()`'s existing `this.urlToPageId()` usage is untouched, TD-030 itself remains open and unfixed. **Verified live** (on top of TD-041's truncation fix, required for OrangeHRM's response to parse at all — see the OrangeHRM-scope-dependency note in Design decisions captured, since `detectFlows()`'s gating meant this had to be exercised by calling `identifyCandidates()`/`enrichWithAi()` directly rather than through the production entry point): both apps show a real mix of `click` steps with real `elementId`s (e.g. `inventory-html:item3ImgLink`, `web-index-php-leave-viewLeaveModule:assignLeave`) and `assert-navigation`+`groundingWarnings` fallbacks for genuinely missing edges, matching the connectivity shape measured in the investigation. `npm run check`: zero errors. |
| TD-040 | `enrichWithAi()` tells the AI the known roles list in its own prompt (`"Known roles: adminUser"`) but never validated that the AI actually respected it. Confirmed live during the TD-031 investigation: OrangeHRM has exactly one role (`adminUser`), yet the AI proposed `roleId: "standardUser"` — a SauceDemo role — for some or all of its proposed flows across multiple real calls. | `8ff028e` | Validates each proposed flow's resolved `roleId` (after the existing `|| 'standardUser'` default) against the same known-roles set passed into the prompt. A `roleId` not in that set gets the flow discarded, logged with the flow id, the bad `roleId`, and the real known-roles list — never a silent drop. Deliberately does not remap a hallucinated `roleId` to a real one (nearest-match guessing is a different, riskier behavior, not built here). **Verified live:** one real OrangeHRM run had 8 of 12 proposed flows hallucinate `"standardUser"` (all 8 rejected, each logged) while the other 3 used the real `adminUser` role and passed through with their compiled steps untouched; a second real run reproduced 15/15 hallucinated and 15/15 correctly rejected. SauceDemo's own valid roles (`standardUser`, `lockedUser`) passed through unaffected in both runs. `npm run check`: zero errors. |
| TD-041 | `enrichWithAi()`'s `maxTokens: 800` truncated OrangeHRM's real response mid-JSON (30 pages, our largest test app) — `JSON.parse()` then threw on the truncated text, and a single bare `catch` silently discarded the entire response via one generic `console.warn`, with no detail on which app, how much was attempted, or why it failed. | `12ba423` | Same root-cause family as the already-resolved `nameWithAi` truncation fix (`48113e8`: raised `maxTokens` + chunked batching) — but `enrichWithAi()`'s output shape (variable-length flow arrays) differs from `nameWithAi`'s (fixed per-batch element list), so that fix doesn't port as-is. Measured OrangeHRM's real, uncapped usage directly rather than guessing: 1120 output tokens for a complete 12-flow response. Raised `maxTokens` to 2500 (~2.2x headroom over the measured value) rather than chunking — chunking would mean the AI only ever sees a subset of pages per call, changing what "the most important flows across the app" can mean for a flow spanning chunk boundaries; revisit if a real app's measured usage ever approaches this cap. Split the single `catch` into two: `aiCall()` itself throwing (network/auth) vs. the response parsing as invalid JSON (truncation or malformed output) are now logged separately, each with the app name, and the parse-failure path additionally logs token usage and a response preview. **Verified live:** OrangeHRM real run — 15 flows parsed successfully, zero truncation (previously failed entirely at `maxTokens: 800`). SauceDemo: 5 flows, no regression. `npm run check`: zero errors. |

All four flows now compile with a real mix of `click` steps (real edge found, e.g. `direct-checkout-flow`'s `shoppingCartLink`/`checkout` clicks) and `assert-navigation` fallbacks *with* populated `groundingWarnings` (no edge found) — exactly TD-031's designed behavior, not the old silent zero-click shape. The remaining 4 of 8 failures across all four flows are `inventory-sidebar-link` not visible — **TD-032**/**TD-033**, unrelated, reproducing identically against fresh data. Fresh crawl/regenerated specs not committed yet — adopting this as the new baseline is a separate decision from this doc cleanup. |

## Resolved (Session 20 — TD-032 heuristic expansion + shared-element dedup)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-032 | `ElementClassifier.determineCritical()` only marked elements critical via 4 narrow rules (data-test, submit/password type, role=button-with-text). OrangeHRM's HIGH (1.00) verification confidence was computed over only 36 of 1223 modeled elements (2.9%) — SauceDemo's near-universal `data-test` instrumentation made this invisible until tested against an app without it. | `e9ba137` | **Design decision: Path 3 (heuristic expansion) was sufficient — the AI-assisted alternative was evaluated and not needed.** Added 3 additive rules: (1) any form input regardless of type, (2) accessible name + interactive tag/role (not just role=button), (3) inside a `<form>`. Measured live before committing: OrangeHRM jumped to 73.7% raw — investigated rather than assumed acceptable, traced to Rule 2 correctly flagging real nav-shell links, just counted once per page across all 30 pages instead of once (none were noise/false positives on spot-check). Added `Crawler.deduplicateSharedElements()`, an app-agnostic cross-page dedup keyed on label+kind+resolved href, link-elements only (generic button labels aren't reliable cross-page identity signals — risks conflating unrelated controls, so deliberately not deduped). **Final numbers, from the real production crawl pipeline (not an approximation script):** OrangeHRM 881/1295 critical raw (68.0%), 469/856 deduped (**54.8%**) — up from the 2.9% baseline, a real, mechanistically-explained increase, not unexplained noise. SauceDemo 131/156 raw (84.0%), 123/144 deduped (85.4%) — modest, expected increase (the burger-menu Open/Close buttons are now correctly critical; not a jump, since SauceDemo's `data-test` coverage already dominated). **Stable suite re-run against both apps' fully regenerated specs, real counts:** 278 passed, 7 failed (6.5m) — traced every failure individually, all 7 map to already-logged, pre-existing tech debt unrelated to this fix (TD-044 ×1, TD-016/017's Add-to-cart gap ×3, TD-030's bare-locator bug ×1, TD-020 ×1, plus one cascading from the Add-to-cart gap) — **zero new failures from the larger critical set.** The new burger-menu-button critical-element check (`#react-burger-menu-btn`) passes cleanly everywhere it's asserted, confirming the new rules don't introduce their own visibility mismatches. Side effects found and logged separately, not silently absorbed: **TD-048** (within-page table-row repetition on OrangeHRM, out of scope here) and **TD-049** (a pre-existing, unrelated `SpecGenerator` cap now masks TD-032/033's original motivating sidebar-link case from generated output — not a regression introduced by this fix, but flagged rather than left implicit). |

## Resolved (Session 21 — TD-049 batched critical-element checks)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-049 | `SpecGenerator.generateFlowTests()`'s critical-elements loop capped at the first 3 critical elements per page (`.slice(0, 3)`) — an undocumented default from `d5cc5a7` (2026-06-16), three weeks before TD-032 ever existed, not a deliberate response to anything. Quantified before fixing, not assumed: this was silently dropping **119 of 131 (90.8%)** of SauceDemo's critical elements and **791 of 881 (89.8%)** of OrangeHRM's, app-wide — not just TD-032/033's one motivating `inventory-sidebar-link` case, which just happened to be the one element whose drop anyone noticed. | `59d78b1` | **Design decision: option (c), batch into fixed-size groups of 10, each its own test case** — chosen over (a) removing the cap entirely (one test with up to 76 sequential assertions means one bad element gives zero signal on the rest in that run) and (b) raising the cap to a larger fixed number (same flaw, just less visible; OrangeHRM's worst pages still exceed 49 critical elements). Batching gives full coverage with no information loss while keeping each test's blast radius bounded. **Verified live:** `inventory-sidebar-link` confirmed back in coverage (batch 1 of 8 on `inventory-html`). OrangeHRM's generated output is unaffected by this specific fix — its only flow (`admin-login`) has zero steps, so the critical-elements loop never executes for it regardless of the cap; a separate, pre-existing constraint (`detectFlows()`'s gating, already noted under TD-031's "OrangeHRM scope dependency" design decision), not something this fix could reach. **Stable suite re-run, real counts: 277 passed, 39 failed, 1 flaky (11.3m).** Every failure traced individually via real error text, not assumed — all 39 resolve to 5 distinct root causes, 4 already known: TD-044 ×1, TD-016/017's Add-to-cart gap ×8, **TD-032/033's `inventory-sidebar-link` ×4** (confirms the restored coverage works — this is the original bug, correctly caught again, not a regression), TD-030 ×1, TD-020 ×1, and **24 occurrences of one genuinely new root cause → TD-050**. The 1 flaky is pre-existing webkit timing flakiness (TD-LOW-002 family), unrelated. No stop condition hit — 5 distinct causes is well within reasonable triage, regardless of the raw 39-test count inflated by redundant per-flow generation. TD-050 (the new root cause) is tracked in the Open table, not here — it's logged-only, not fixed. |

## Resolved (Session 23 — TD-052 CI schema migration)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-052 | The entire DB schema was missing in CI, so every AI-pipeline DB write hit `SqliteError: no such table: <name>`. All 14 tables (incl. `ai_usage`, `ai_triage`, `runs`, `trends`, `heal_events`, `flaky_analysis`) are created solely by Kysely migrations (`src/core/storage/migrations/001_initial_schema.ts`), applied only via `npm run db:migrate` (`src/core/storage/migrate.ts`) — `src/core/storage/db.ts` does no lazy table creation. The CI workflow (`.github/workflows/e2e-pipeline.yml`) had no migrate step, and `forge-framework.db` is gitignored (untracked + listed in `.gitignore`), so each CI run started from an empty DB with zero tables. **Accuracy note carried forward from the open entry:** the `ai_usage` insert lives in `AiClient.aiCall()`'s `finally` block (after the API call) and its `SqliteError` was caught and swallowed as a `console.warn` — so the missing table silently disabled AI cost/usage recording in CI but did not by itself fail triage. The all-`Unknown` triage result is caused by the separate, still-open **TD-053**; this fix does not change that. | `02e8505` | Design pre-approved by Aiden. Single-step workflow change: added a `Run database migrations` step (`npm run db:migrate`) immediately after `Install dependencies` (`npm ci`) and before the test run / AI pipeline steps, so the schema exists before anything reads or writes the DB. Fixes the missing-table error for **every** DB-touching pipeline step (results-store → `runs`/`trends`, flaky-predictor → `flaky_analysis`, HealStore → `heal_events`, triage → `ai_triage`, AiClient → `ai_usage`), not just `ai_usage`. **Idempotency verified locally** — `npm run db:migrate` against the already-migrated local DB reports `[migration] Already up to date.` and exits clean, confirming `migrateToLatest()` only applies un-applied migrations (safe on every run). No corruption risk: CI's DB is a fresh gitignored file each run. **Not yet confirmed in a live CI run** (secrets/container env) — held per the task's explicit "do not push" stop condition; a real CI run is still the final confirmation. **TD-053 remains open** (no timeout/retry resilience + missing unknown-rate gate); this entry resolves only the schema-creation gap. |

## Resolved (Session 24 — TD-053 retry wrapper + unknown-rate gate)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-053 | Anthropic API calls in the CI AI pipeline were not resilient to mid-stream connection drops: every triage `aiCall` failed with `FetchError: ... Premature close` and all 39 calls returned `Unknown`/`Low`. The SDK's built-in retry (default `maxRetries: 2`) only wraps the fetch-to-response-headers phase (`makeRequest`, `core.js:301-319`) and returns the unread response at `:329`; a `Premature close` during body streaming is thrown outside that envelope and is never retried. Compounding it, `ai-triage.ts` caught each per-call failure into an `Unknown/Low` verdict and `main()` exited `0` even at a 100% failure rate, so an all-`Unknown` CI run looked like success. | `6f2e36a` | Design pre-approved by Aiden. **CHANGE 1 (`AiClient.ts`):** app-level retry inside `aiCall()` — 3 attempts (initial + 2 retries); retries only on `Anthropic.APIConnectionError` (incl. its timeout subclass), `FetchError`, or a message matching `/premature close/i` — never on a real `APIError` (4xx/5xx); exponential backoff `1000 * 2^attempt + jitter(0–500ms)`; each attempt logged `[AiClient] Retry attempt {n}/3 after {error}`. Timeout 90s set on the shared client. **Added beyond the literal param list (flagged for Aiden): `maxRetries: 0` on the client** so the app-level wrapper is the single retry authority and "3 attempts maximum" holds exactly — otherwise the SDK's default 2 retries would compound. **Per-attempt `ai_usage` recording:** each attempt writes its own row (own `duration_ms`/`success`) with a new `retry_attempt` column (0 = first attempt). Migration **007_retry_attempt.ts** adds the column idempotently (swallows duplicate-column on re-run, cross-dialect); `AiUsageTable` (`storage/types.ts`) gained `retry_attempt: number` so the insert type-checks. **CHANGE 2 (`ai-triage.ts`):** unknown-rate gate at end of `main()` — `process.exit(1)` when API-failure Unknowns (reasoning contains `API call failed`, not genuinely-unclassifiable) are ≥ `UNKNOWN_RATE_THRESHOLD` (0.80) of total **and** total ≥ `UNKNOWN_FLOOR` (5); logs count/total/percentage. Both constants named at top of file. **Verified locally:** `npm run check` green; `npm run db:migrate` applied 007 (`retry_attempt` column confirmed present); `npx tsx src/pipeline/ai-triage.ts` against the existing 39-failure `reports/test-results.json` produced **real verdicts (34 Bug / 2 Environment / 3 Flaky / 0 Unknown), exit 0** — gate correctly did not fire; 39 `ai_usage` rows recorded, all `retry_attempt=0, success=1`. **Not exercised live:** the retry path itself (no way to induce a `Premature close` locally) — verified by structure + compile only. **Not yet confirmed in a live CI run** — held per the task's "do not push" stop condition. Builds on TD-052 (schema must exist in CI for the per-attempt recording to land). |

---

## Resolved (Session 25 — TD-051 DB-shape rewrite, Option A)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-051 | `trend-analysis.ts` (`buildSummary`, `:108`) and `release-notes.ts` (`analyseRuns`, `:158`) both crashed with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'passRate')` — they consumed the old JSON-file `RunHistory` shape (`run.stats.passRate`, `run.stats.flaky`, camelCase `run.durationMs`, per-run `failures[]`/`flakyTests[]`), but `RunRepository.findByApp()` returns flat snake_case `RunsTable` rows (`passed`, `failed`, `duration_ms`; no `flaky`/`pass_rate`). Audit also found the blast radius was far wider than the two crash lines, plus an inverted-ordering bug (`findByApp` is DESC, both scripts assumed ascending) and that the per-test detail both scripts depend on has **no DB source** (`test_results` is never populated by any production path; `TrendsTable` is per-day, not per-run). | `75713e8`, `42dee6b` | **Option A (DB-only, visible stubs)** per Aiden's sign-off informed by the audit. Both scripts rewritten to consume `RunsTable` directly via a `toRunSummary()` mapper: `pass_rate` computed as `passed / total_tests * 100` (guards `total_tests === 0`) — **not** from `TrendsTable` (wrong granularity/semantic); `findByApp` DESC result reversed to chronological ascending before all latest/slice/streak/first-vs-second-half logic. No-data-source sections (per-test risk tiers, unstable-tests table, per-run failure drill-down, flaky counts, browser-bias, new/resolved-failure deltas) are **stubbed visibly** (`Per-test … not available … see TD-056`) rather than rendered as misleading empty/zero data; release-notes' Claude prompt explicitly states per-test detail is absent (not zero) and forbids fabricating test IDs. `release-notes` health score is now run-level pass-rate + trend only (new/resolved deltas dropped). **Verified locally:** `npm run check` green; `npx tsx src/pipeline/trend-analysis.ts` → 14 runs, real pass rates (87.4% current / 92.7% avg), wrote `trend-dashboard.html` + `trend-report.md` with the stub rendered (no crash); `npx tsx src/pipeline/release-notes.ts` → 2-run window, avg 91.8%, wrote md/html/json with the stub in Risk Areas / Failures / Recommended Actions and **no fabricated test IDs**. Two scoped commits. **Out of scope, logged not fixed:** `knowledge-query.ts` has the identical bug (+ 3 unconfirmed suspects) → **TD-056**. The underlying "`test_results` is never populated" gap is the root cause behind the stubs (also TD-056's scope). Scripts' CI `continue-on-error` (kept under TD-042 pending this fix) can now be revisited — separate decision. |

## Resolved (Session 26 — TD-059 reports-cache clobber)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-059 | **Prime root cause of stale CI run-data.** `e2e-pipeline.yml`'s `Save reports cache` step used `actions/cache@v4` (whose main action *restores*) with a static, immutable key `reports-${{ github.ref_name }}` = `reports-main`, frozen at June 4. Every run it got a cache hit and restored the frozen `reports/` over the freshly-written `reports/test-results.json` — **after** the test step, **before** the `forge-reports` artifact upload — so Job 2 consumed a 3-week-old `test-results.json` (confirmed: run `28227767210`, June-26 tests wrote `282 passed / 39 failed`, then `Cache restored from key: reports-main` clobbered it; Job 2 read `runId 2026-06-04T15-00-40`). The AI pipeline was triaging/storing ~3-week-old data while reporting success. | `3cfbe28` | Design pre-approved by Aiden: **delete** the cache rather than repair it (the `forge-reports` artifact is the real Job1→Job2 handoff; `run-history.json`/`trends.json` persist via the auto-commit to main). **Safety check before deleting (all passed):** (1) no step in either job consumes the cache as input — Job 1 uploads read fresh test-step output; Job 2 readers read the `forge-reports` artifact; `run-history.json`/`trends.json` come from the git checkout; (2) `Restore reports cache` existed only to feed the deleted `Save` and to carry `run-history.json` the checkout already provides, so it was removed too; (3) `run-history.json`/`trends.json` still reach Job 2 via **two** surviving paths (committed git checkout + the `forge-reports` artifact). **Removed BOTH** the `Restore reports cache` and `Save reports cache` steps; nothing else changed. **Verified:** clean 16-line-deletion diff, zero remaining reports-cache references, Job 1 lifecycle now write→upload with nothing restoring in between, `npm run check` green. **Not yet confirmed live** — held per the task's "do not push" stop condition; the agreed confirmation is one CI run showing Results Store logs a fresh (June-26) runId, not a stale one. Sequenced first; **TD-057** (dedup-gated insert), **TD-058** (ephemeral CI DB), **TD-060** (source-of-truth) remain open and follow. |

## Resolved (Session 27 — TD-061 SDK native-fetch transport)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-061 | Anthropic SDK `0.32.1`'s bundled **`node-fetch` (HTTP/1.1)** deterministically throws `FetchError: Premature close` on the CI bare `ubuntu-latest` runner (Node 24 / Linux). On the **same runner with the same key**, raw `curl` (HTTP/2) and Node **native `fetch`** (undici) both return **200**. This is the true root cause of all-`Unknown` triage in CI — **not** container egress (TD-053 mis-attributed it; it fails on the bare runner too), and **not** auth/rate-limit/egress (curl returned 200 with `anthropic-ratelimit-requests-remaining: 4999`). The TD-053 retry wrapper can't help because every node-fetch attempt premature-closes deterministically. | `3071dfa` | Diagnosed on throwaway branch **`diag/premature-close`** (runs `28249858033` / `28250081563`): curl=200, native fetch=200, SDK node-fetch=Premature close on all 6 calls including the single isolated one (`status=undefined`). Fix: route the SDK through `globalThis.fetch` in `AiClient.ts` (one added option; preserves `maxRetries: 0` + 90s timeout; TD-053 retry wrapper untouched). **Verified locally:** `npm run check` green; `npx tsx src/pipeline/ai-triage.ts` produced real verdicts (33 Bug / 2 Environment / 4 Flaky / 0 Unknown), zero "Premature close". **Not yet confirmed in CI** — held per the "do not push" stop condition; the agreed confirmation is one CI run showing real triage verdicts instead of Premature close. SDK upgrade follow-up logged as **TD-062**; the `diag/premature-close` branch is kept unmerged as insurance until a clean CI run confirms. |

## Resolved (Session 32 — TD-066 evidence-tier flow confidence + honesty markers)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |

| TD-066 | **[HIGH — unearned confidence]** Confidence/score values were emitted as hardcoded literals, not derived from evidence: `FlowDetector` assigned `confidence: 0.99 / 0.9 / 0.70` regardless of actual grounding; AI-triage stamped a fallback `'Medium'` indistinguishable from a model-returned value; `HealStore` wrote a fabricated `1.0` on every heal. Dedup now ranks on **real evidence** (observed-grounding ratio, step presence, step count), not the label. Triage gained `confidenceSource` (`'model'`\|`'fallback'`) marking fallbacks in markdown/DB (new `ai_triage.confidence_source` column, migration `008`). Healing stops fabricating `1.0` — recovers the real vision confidence that was being silently dropped (plumbed `HealStoreEntry.confidence`), emits a `-1` unverified sentinel for strategy-chain heals with no signal. Schema + 3 committed models migrated deterministically (11/11 flows, 0 validation errors). **Proof:** by-construction test `scripts/verify-td066.test.ts` (7/7 pass, `npm run test:unit`), vacuity-checked — each assertion fails against the old `0.99`/`1.0`. Smoking gun now impossible **three ways**: derivation logic, committed data, and the guarded test. **Scope deferred (not regressions):** heal-**correctness** derivation → **TD-065** (this only stopped the `1.0` fabrication, added no correctness signal); ElementClassifier strategy-tier priors (→ **TD-088** rename) and `coverage-gap.ts` `?? 50` (→ **TD-089**) left as-is — they are fixed reliability priors / dormant unshipped code, not source-type-constant fabrications. ## Resolved (Session 37 — TD-093 Bootstrap Mode Phase 1)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-093 | Bootstrap Mode — zero-config onboarding from a URL. Previously a hand-authored `onboarding.<app>.config.ts` had to exist before `npm run onboard` could run; the detection intelligence existed (StrategyDetector, auth-form detection) but ran only *inside* the crawl and was never emitted as a reusable config. appType had NO live inference at all (config-only). | `86d5a5c` (Commit 1 detection core), `fe6e74a` (Commit 2 CLI wiring + write), `cba7c5c` (Commit 3 --dry-run), + this commit (Commit 4 manifest + close) | **Fix:** `Bootstrap` (`src/core/onboarding/Bootstrap.ts`) — given a URL + credentials, detects **appType** (DOM SPA-framework signals; net-new — no prior live inference), **crawlStrategy** (reuses StrategyDetector), **authType/loginUrl** (password-field count, PageVisitor's signal), and **appName** (hostname SLD or `--name`). Every value is a `DetectedField` carrying **DetectionConfidence** (`high`/`medium`/`low`) + human-readable `source` — never invents values; conservative defaults + `AUTO-DETECTED` annotations when uncertain. `generateConfig()` emits a typed `onboarding.<app>.config.ts` string; `writeConfig()` writes it with an **overwrite guard** (aborts if the file exists unless `--force`, which warns — protects curated configs from silent clobber). CLI: `--bootstrap --url=… --role/--username/--password (multi-role) --name --maxPages --force --dry-run`, intercepted before `resolveConfig` so the generated file is then found + crawled. `--dry-run` previews config + manifest and exits (no write, no crawl). `bootstrap-manifest-<app>.json` records the detection run (role names only — **never passwords**; gitignored). **Portability (TD-097):** all paths runtime-derived from `__dirname`/`REPO_ROOT` via `path.join`/`path.relative`, forward-slash-normalized — no hardcoded paths (`generateRunId` inlined rather than imported from `evals/` to avoid an `src`→`evals` rootDir break of `check:core`). **Proof:** live-verified on SauceDemo (detect `spa`/`bfs`/`form-login`, dry-run preview exit 0, overwrite guard exit 1 / `--force` overwrite, manifest write) + `npm run check` green. **Phase 2 deferred:** multi-step auth, SSO/OAuth, goal-directed exploration (alongside **TD-013** agentic crawl). **Note:** the commit hashes above are pre-push; a `git pull --rebase` before push will rewrite them (same as prior closes) — reconcile after push if needed. |

## Resolved (TD-110 — Hydration-Aware Auth Detection + Cumulative Observation)

| ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-110 | Root cause (from the read-only diagnostic — NOT the depth issue originally hypothesized): `detectAuthType` counted `input[type="password"]` immediately at domcontentloaded; OrangeHRM's Vue-rendered login form hadn't hydrated → `authType 'none'` → role `authFlow 'none'` → AuthManager treated it as guest → crawl found 1 page (the login page) in 9.6s. **Fix 1 (ObservationSettlingPolicy):** `detectAuthType()` now settles before counting — `SPA_AUTH_SETTLING_POLICY` waits for `input[type='password']` with a 3s OUTER BOUND (evidence-driven, not a fixed sleep — Nova Q2; settles instantly when the field exists; runs concurrently with detectCrawlStrategy's 2s wait inside the same Promise.all ≈ 1s worst-case wall cost). Reusable interface + `DomcontentloadedPolicy`/`WaitForSelectorPolicy`/`NetworkIdlePolicy`/`ComposedPolicy`; future consumers: nav/modal/shadow-DOM/React-suspense/Vue-hydration detection. **Fix 2 (`applyAuthTypeObservation`, exported pure function):** authType corrects on OBSERVATION of a login control (auth goal synthesized), independent of auth outcome — authType = "does the form exist?" (observation), authOutcome = "did login work?" (result), never coupled (Nova Q3). `upgradeField()` only touched confidence — Fix 2 corrects the VALUE (`'none'` guard only; form-login/high never touched; observation → medium, achievement → high, composed). | ID | Description | Resolved in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-015 | `lockedUser` role auth "occasional failure" on SauceDemo triaged (read-only, Session 7). **Not** a framework auth bug and **not** a live-site account-lockout defect. The lockout works correctly: TC069's lockout assertions — `getErrorMessage()` → `'Epic sadface: Sorry, this user has been locked out'`, and the post-login `not.toContain('inventory.html')` — pass reliably, so the account IS being correctly rejected. The intermittent red came from TC069's *direct-navigation* URL assertions (`expect(url).not.toContain('inventory.html'/'cart.html'/'checkout-step-one.html')`) racing SauceDemo's client-side redirect: SauceDemo keeps the protected URL and renders an access-denied banner rather than redirecting, so `goto()` resolves before any redirect and the URL check flakes. A test defect, not an auth defect. | fe08b1d | Triage only — no `AuthManager` change. Re-filed the actual defect as **TD-107** (TC069 direct-nav assertion). The lockedUser account is genuinely locked *and* FORGE handles it correctly; the only fix needed is the TC069 assertion correction. See TD-107. |

| ID | Description | Priority-at-close | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TD-UI-061 | **`run-history.json` `passRate` string shows a rosy rate for an input-unhealthy (could-not-verify) run — phantom-green surviving in the FILE layer, and it is read LIVE.** The ADR-018 red-side milestone closed phantom-green at the DB pass-rate (`runs.status`→`'unknown'`; `TrendRepository`/`getPassRateSince` exclude it from the numerator). But the legacy `run-history.json` per-run `stats.passRate` string (`results-store.ts` `buildRunRecord`, `(passed+flaky)/total`) does NOT consult `input_health`/`status`: an input-unhealthy run whose tests passed (stale/no-provenance/degraded input, 0 failures) still writes e.g. `'100.0%'`. **This is READ LIVE, not a demoted event-log:** `release-notes.ts:98,151` reads the file and averages `r.passRate` into `avgPassRate`/`passRateTrend` (release scoring); `platform-server.ts:190,570` uses `run-history.json` as the dashboard's *"Preferred source … same numbers the dashboard shows"* and renders it (`platform.js:341,347`, `dashboard-server.ts:74,137`). So the same phantom-green fixed at the DB layer survives one layer over, in the human/release-facing displays. (Scope note: the *heal-unresolved-test* case does NOT leak green here — those tests drag the numerator down; the leak is specifically the input-health branch of could-not-verify, TD-UI-047's original class.) Remedy (NOT now): the file's per-run `passRate` — and every live reader that averages/displays it — must treat an input-unhealthy / could-not-verify run the way the DB readers do (exclude from the pass numerator, or surface the run as could-not-verify), OR the file readers migrate to the DB (`RunRepository`/`TrendRepository`) as the single source (the DB-is-source-of-truth direction, TD-117). **Live phantom-green: run-history.json passRate is read as a DISPLAYED pass-rate by release-notes.ts (release scoring), dashboard-server.ts/platform-server.ts/platform.js (dashboard 'preferred source') — an input-unhealthy run shows a rosier rate than the DB/trends honest value. Same phantom-green class the ADR-018 DB pipe just fixed, surviving in the file layer. NEXT milestone after the DB pipe lands — not deferred.** | ✅ RESOLVED (surgical bridge) | ADR-018 red-side milestone (2026-07-16); resolved 2026-07-16 via a surgical bridge fix (hash + CI pending — not yet committed). **✅ RESOLVED (surgical bridge).** The live green-on-empty health lie in the deprecated **src/platform** dashboard is neutralized surgically. The ONE real lie was `dashboard-server.ts` `overallHealth` falling through to `'Healthy'` (green) on absent evidence — now gated in the extracted pure seam **`src/platform/healthState.ts`** (`resolveOverallHealth`: no evidence OR a latest run FORGE could-not-verify [`status='unknown'`] → `'Unknown'`, rendered **neutral grey**, label **"Insufficient Evidence"** — never green, never a number, never `'Failing'`; absence ≠ failure). Extracted to a seam because `dashboard-server.ts` starts a server at import (untestable directly). The TRUE honest replacement (not a bridge) = **TD-UI-062 (HIGH)**. Permanent fix remains TD-UI-062.** |
| TD-UI-058 | **ADR-018 RED-SIDE: could-not-verify collapsed into `failed` at two aggregation boundaries (phantom-RED).** The Verify LIEs milestone (`4a3573c`) closed phantom-GREEN; these are the inverse — a state FORGE could-not-verify recorded as a demonstrated FAILURE (a phantom-red is the same dishonesty inverted, ADR-018 §Consequences). **C1 (Heal):** `SmartLocator.resolve` (`SmartLocator.ts:100-124`) is binary `heal | throw` — a sub-threshold/uncertain Vision heal (`visionResult.success===false`) or an unresolvable healed selector `throw`s, propagating into `VerificationRunner.executeStep`'s catch → `failedAtStep` → the flow aggregates to `failed`. "Could-not-heal-confidently" is could-not-verify, not a demonstrated defect. **C2 (Ingestion):** `normalizeStatus` (`testResultExtraction.ts:40`) `default: return 'failed'` for any unrecognized Playwright status vocab — the enum `'passed'|'failed'|'flaky'|'skipped'` has no `'unknown'`/`'could-not-verify'`, so an unmapped status (e.g. a future `timedout`/`interrupted` reaching this path) is laundered into a demonstrated failure and flows into every `status`-based tally. | Medium | Audit #2 (2026-07-15), findings C1+C2. Governed by ADR-018 (Aggregate to the Weakest Truth) — the RED-side counterpart to `4a3573c`'s green-side. **Scope as the ADR-018 red-side milestone WITH TD-UI-047** (the run→pass-rate rollup is the third aggregation site). C1 remedy: a `could-not-heal` outcome distinct from a hard miss (+ ADR-016 remedy: provide a stable selector / allow agentic exploration). C2 remedy: add `'unknown'`/`'could-not-verify'` to the status vocabulary (or a `status_raw` column). Docs-only; batch with the milestone, NOT solo. **✅ RESOLVED `a0c57e2`** (CI run 29512536322). C1: `SmartLocator.resolve` now emits `HealUnresolvedError` + a structured `forge:could-not-verify` Playwright annotation (single choke point, `resolve()`) + `recordUnresolved` failure persistence (distinct from promotion-shaped `recordHeal` — closes the archetype-4 winners-only gap). C2: `normalizeStatus` gains `'could-not-verify'`; `timedOut`/`interrupted`/unknown-default → could-not-verify (the reversed phantom-red); dual re-grade (streaming + batch) with failed-dominates precedence. Run: `deriveRunOutcome` routes could-not-verify → `runs.status 'unknown'` (results-store authoritative w/ `input_health`; reporter partial → TD-UI-060). **CORRECTION (from channel-verification):** C1's original downstream trace was WRONG — `VerificationRunner` does NOT use `SmartLocator`; the heal throw lands in the executing spec → `normalizeStatus`, so C1 and C2 are the SAME pipe (not two independent landings). | TD-033 | `VerificationRunner.verifyElement()` (`VerificationRunner.ts:381`) certifies elements via DOM attached state, while generated specs assert strict `toBeVisible()`. Related to but distinct from TD-032: TD-032 is about which elements get checked at all; TD-033 is about the check criteria disagreeing once an element is checked. **Attempted this session, discarded (not committed):** implemented the literal fix this entry describes (`'attached'` → `'visible'` in `verifyElement()`'s `waitFor`). Root cause traced, not assumed: see **TD-047**, which supersedes this entry's diagnosis (the gap isn't the `attached`/`visible` predicate choice, it's `VerificationRunner`'s navigation pattern). TD-033 stays Open — the originating report is still valid, just not fixable by the change it literally describes. **Design decision (Option D, signed off by Raj):** decided not to fix the underlying navigation/check-criteria gap. Instead redefined what verification claims to guarantee, and added a report flag so the limitation is visible rather than implicit — see the new `verificationTier` field on `ElementResult`/`FlowResult` (`VerificationRunner.ts`) and the cross-referenced "Design decisions captured" entry below. The sidebar-link case still shows `status: "passed"`, but now also `verificationTier: "dom-presence"` rather than an unqualified pass. **Update (TD-032 close-out, this session):** the underlying mismatch this entry describes is still real and unfixed, but `inventory-sidebar-link` itself no longer appears in any regenerated SauceDemo spec's "critical elements visible" check at all — not because it stopped being critical (it's still `critical: true` in the model), but because of an unrelated interaction with `SpecGenerator`'s pre-existing 3-element cap; see **TD-049**. This entry's own gap is therefore currently unexercised by generated output, not resolved.  **RESOLVED — FC-003/TD-082 lineage (`7bd1362`/`ec6a1ab`): generator asserts per observedState (SpecGenerator:424-432, assertionHelpers:66 toBeAttached vs toBeVisible); the verify-vs-spec mismatch no longer exists.** |

| TD-UI-028 | The model lies about its own provenance. `CrawlRunner:217` persists an `app-model.json` with `crawledAt`, `crawlDurationMs`, and `classificationRunId` ALL SET even when the crawl discovered 0 pages, 0 flows, and 0 endpoints. | TD-UI-027 | FORGE cannot detect when RE-ONBOARDING is required. Onboard establishes the app's identity (appType, authType, crawl strategy, credential slot); crawl establishes its contents. Content changes need only a re-crawl — but IDENTITY changes (MPA→SPA re-platform, form-login→SSO/token auth change, base-URL move) invalidate the onboarding config itself. Today FORGE has no detection for this: if an app switches to SSO, the crawl simply fails at auth with a confusing error rather than saying "your authType is stale — re-onboard." Same class as TD-067: FORGE is blind to the staleness of its own inputs. | Medium | Surfaced by Raj 2026-07-12 while designing the Crawl-tab freshness UX. Needs a design pass: what signals would prove an onboarding config is stale, and can bootstrap detection be re-run cheaply to compare?  **RESOLVED (2026-07-18) — identity-divergence detection shipped (TD-UI-027 implementation). Failure-triggered probe (shouldProbeIdentity) re-observes authType/appType/baseUrl via the already-shipped detectors and emits a three-outcome (divergence-detected / no-divergence-detected / inconclusive) machine-readable diagnostic into the EXISTING crawlDiagnostics channel (reason 'identity-divergence'); inconclusive can never collapse into no-divergence-detected. New: IdentityDivergence.ts + Crawler.ts hook + schema reason-enum. D1-D8 green; 509 unit.
